# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The complete self-contained proof is in CONDITIONAL_ENERGY_LEDGER.md sections 1-5; CORRELATED_AUXILIARY_CONSTRUCTION.tex is a concise mathematical companion. Its key formulas are:

For any bent branch on d data bits, sigma_t(x)=2^(d/2) ghat_x(t) is Boolean and P(S_X=v,S_Y=t)=2^(-d) sigmahat_t(v)^2. Hence H(F)=d+E_t H(sigma_t), I_Y=d/2, I_X=E_t I(sigma_t), B_X=E_t H(sigma_t), and H(S_Y|S_X)=d-I(S_X;S_Y)<=2I_Y. Magnitude energy is zero; signed phase energy is exactly I_X.

A fully realizable arbitrary-row family is F(x,a,b)=(-1)^(a dot b)h_b(x) on k+2m bits. Its signed coefficient is 2^(-m)(-1)^(s dot t)hhat_s(v). Thus H(F)=2m+E_s H(h_s), I_Y=m, I_X=E_s I(h_s), and H(S_Y|S_X)=2m-I(V;S). This retains the entire conditional auxiliary remainder.

The linear control h_b(x)=(-1)^(x dot b_[k]) has H=2m, I_X=k/2, I_Y=m, H(S_Y|S_X)=2m-k, and distinct branches with dependent spectral marginals. At k=1 the conditional auxiliary entropy divided by phase energy diverges as 4m-2, although H/I<2. Therefore a selector-phase-only bound on the auxiliary remainder fails.

For an arbitrary k-bit seed g and m>=k+1, add a separate rare B-point tag to each selector assignment: h_b(x)=g(x)(-1)^(1{b=tag(x)}). Every branch is bent, distinct even modulo sign, and the spectral marginals are dependent. With delta=2^(k-m), epsilon=2^(2-m), the exact ledger is B_X=(1-delta)H(g)+2^(-m)sum_u H(g^(u)), p_X=(1-epsilon)p_g+epsilon Uniform, I_X=I(g)+2^(1-m)(k-2I(g)). Entropy continuity has the explicit analytic error bounds |B_X-H(g)|<=delta k and 0<I(V;S)<=h2(epsilon)+(epsilon+delta)k. Thus B_X/I_X tends to H(g)/I(g) for every fixed nonconstant seed.

Consequently a uniform B_X<=K I_X on this distinct, correlated family implies FEI(K) for arbitrary seeds; conversely FEI(K) applied to each Boolean phase row supplies that bound. The phase completion remains full-FEI difficulty. The full tagged family itself has H/I<3 because m>=k+1: this is a conditional-payment scope guard, not a disproof.

First unsupported line: assert that an absolute finite K bounds E_s H(h_s) by K E_s I(h_s) using only Booleanity, flat data magnitudes, branch distinctness and spectral dependence. Arbitrary seed entropy survives all four hypotheses.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Complete derivation: DERIVATION.md sections 1-8, with a concise standalone mathematical companion CORRELATED_AUXILIARY_CONSTRUCTION.tex.

For k selector bits, m=ceil(log2(k+1)), N=2^m, assign c_b=e_b at k distinct B-values and c_b=0 otherwise. For ANY Boolean seed g define F_g(x,a,b)=(-1)^(a dot b+c_b dot x)g(x). Its signed coefficient is 2^-m(-1)^(s dot t)ghat(v+c_s). Every physical selector branch is bent and distinct even modulo sign. Exact H=2m+H(g), I_Y=m, I_X=I(g)+(k-2I(g))/N, H(S_X|S_Y)=H(g), and H(S_Y|S_X)=2m-I(S_X;S_Y). Marginal q(v)=(1-k/N)p_g(v)+N^-1 sum_i p_g(v+e_i).

For g_t=z product of t disjoint copies of any nonconstant seed f on n bits, k_t=tn+1 and m_t=ceil(log2(k_t+1)), this gives H(F_t)=tH(f)+2m_t, I(F_t)=tI(f)+J_t with m_t<J_t<m_t+2. The added z spectral bit detects its single tag, so I(S_X;S_Y)>=h2(1/N_t)>0. Thus actual full H(F_t)/I(F_t) tends to H(f)/I(f), with an explicit O(log t/t) error bound. The first stage's tags used m>=k+1 and bounded the FULL H/I below 3; this new logarithmic overhead preserves the FULL seed ratio instead. It is not a disproof because the arbitrary seed ratio remains unpaid.

For EVERY bent-branch address selector, the Boolean phase rows sigma_t satisfy H=d+E H(sigma_t), I=d/2+E I(sigma_t). A ratio above any C>=2 descends to a smaller actual Boolean phase-row function. Therefore flat bent magnitudes do not generate a counterexample from bounded-ratio cores. Combined with the explicit strict-dependence embedding, the optimal constant on distinct correlated bent-branch selectors equals the universal optimal constant, possibly infinity.

For arbitrary F=(-1)^(a dot b)h_b(x), aggregate erasure transports as B(F)=m+E B(h_b)+A, A=sum_j H(S_j|V,S_-j) in [0,m]. A physical b-first paired stopping tree spends internal erasure m, JS m and removed influence m, leaving E H(h_b), E B(h_b), E I(h_b) intact. An a-first split instead creates actual two-row cores G_s(x,z)=h_(s,z)(x), with B_a=1-E JS_z(G_s) and JS_a=1-E B_z(G_s). This identifies the precise colliding/nonflat boundary; it does not price a rare core query by a fixed multiple of its influence.

A034's theorem HOLDS at scope. The noncharacter code seeds fail its unit-row assumption because an actual partial row has intermediate magnitude. Character seed corner admission is exactly the affinity of the zero-code support. The earlier point tags have one admitted corner k=1,m=2; other cases fail unit amplitude or affine support. Thus these embeddings do not refute A034's genuinely structural theorem.

First unsupported universal line: extend the smaller-Boolean-phase payment to nonflat branch amplitudes, whose normalized selector rows can be zero or have multiple magnitudes, while retaining their entire conditional and terminal entropy. Alternatively asserting a finite absolute phase-core payment at the leaves is simply full FEI.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

DERIVATION.md supplies a dimension-independent proof. Quadratic squared spectra
are uniform affine laws of even polar rank. Their dyadic ranks and exact overlap
give the full selector entropy increment; the correlation takes values zero or
signed powers of one half. Enumerating those correlation/rank regimes proves
the sharp decrement. Keeping the physical influence of terminal phases sharpens
the full-function constant to Cstar.

The actual equality case G=(-1)^(zy0y1) has H=4-(9/8)log2(3), I_z=1/4,
I_y=1/2. For the physical split X=z,Y=y, its conditional auxiliary entropy is
2-(3/8)log2(3), so its conditional ratio exceeds two. The admitted distinct-label
f(x,z,y)=(-1)^(xz+zy0y1) restricts at x=0 to G. Thus unrestricted collision closure
with constant two fails; A034 v1 itself remains valid at its stated scope.

First unsupported universal line: unequal affine-cell supports, or repeated
collisions whose phases cease to be quadratic, admit a uniform physical
separator payment after retaining terminal costs. No such bridge is proved here.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

DERIVATION.md gives the full proof. The inverse quadratic branch laws have flat
affine physical supports with integer height constraints. Disjoint supports
force amplitudes1 or2; proper intersections force both amplitudes1; equal
supports permit only the ratio-three nonfair case. A4:1 row requires at least
three fair frequency coordinates. For the forbidden smaller dimensions every
sign branch is automatically quadratic, so this exclusion holds for arbitrary
Fourier signs. Integer Walsh divisibility gives universal weight caps5/8 or17/32.

For EVERY two-address Boolean function, without product/phase assumptions,
H(w)<=E H_1/2(p)+2I_X. Fourier inversion pays each row's address mass; mixed-only
rows all have weight delta/4 and their extra cost is paid once by I_X>=delta.
This retains both H(w) and the complete H(S_Y|S_X). Under the fixed-ratio
admission, the regular rows pay the joint and conditional bills from physical
row influence. The ratio-three zero-fair-coordinate endpoint uses the actual

leaves only ratio3 for the zero-fair-coordinate endpoint. Odd norm divisibility

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

For G=g^tensor(t), p=(1-(E g)^t)/2, a=2^-k, F=1+A(G-1), the complete coefficients give I(F)/a=tI(g)+2kp and H(F)/a=tH(g)+8kp+error, |error|<=32. The output entropy satisfies h2(ap)/a=kp+c with 0<=c<=3. The discarded empty-spectral entropy satisfies h2(Var(F))/a=4kp+error, |error|<=16. All bounds are uniform in the tensor count. Since p>=(1-|E g|)/2>0, their normalized errors vanish for every k->infinity schedule; the gap denominator is strictly positive once k>3/p_*.

Writing R=H(g)/I(g), lambda=lim tI(g)/k, the respective limits are (lambda R+4)/(lambda+1), (lambda R+4)/(lambda+1/2), and (lambda R+2)/(lambda+1); at infinity they all equal R. Bounded or oscillating t at lambda0 gives4,8,2 without assuming the tensor becomes balanced. Thus the largest limiting ratios are max(R,4), max(R,8), max(R,2), all attainable with bias and influence tending to zero. Bounded selector width and growing tensor count instead converge to R. This closes the entire named fixed-seed amplification mechanism, rather than another finite parameter range.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

**Restriction identities and noncircular JS proof.** For a physical coordinate, write u_T=fhat(T), v_T=fhat(T+i), s_T=u_T^2+v_T^2. Child coefficients are u_T+v_T and u_T-v_T. Their squared-law average is s_T. The spectral entropy chain rule gives B_i=sum_T s_T h2(v_T^2/s_T), while subtracting child entropies gives Delta_i H=B_i+JS_i. Physical edge averaging gives I=I_i+avg I(children). Conditioning decreases entropy, so sum_i B_i<=H. These identities are a separate primitive node containing no JS upper bound.

For 0<=z<1 let d(z)=((1+z)ln(1+z)+(1-z)ln(1-z))/2 and r(z)=1-sqrt(1-z^2). Then d'(z)=atanh(z), r'(z)=z/sqrt(1-z^2), and

    (r'-d')'=(1-z^2)^(-3/2)-(1-z^2)^(-1)>=0.

Both derivative difference and value difference start at zero. Integrating twice gives d<=r; evenness and continuity handle negative z and endpoints. On a packet with s>0 use z=2uv/s. Its JS is s*d(z)/ln2, bounded by [s-sqrt(s^2-4u^2v^2)]/ln2=2min(u^2,v^2)/ln2. Summing proves the exact B17-JS-PACKET-SCALAR statement and JS_i<=(2/ln2)min(I_i,1-I_i). The graph does not use the old BASE-ERASURE node, which already contains this inequality, as a premise proving it; it rebuilds BASE-ERASURE from the separate primitive plus the newly reconstructed scalar lemma.

**Selectable bridge.** For a fixed n, K_sel(n) is nondecreasing in n. Every nonconstant f with at most n relevant variables has a relevant i with B_i<=K_sel(n)I_i. The JS lemma yields Delta_i H<=(K_sel(n)+2/ln2)I_i. Restricting reduces the number of relevant variables, so induction with the same constant and exact influence decomposition proves C(n)<=K_sel(n)+2/ln2. Conversely the influence-weighted average of B_i/I_i is B/I<=H/I; the minimum is no larger. Maximizing gives K_sel(n)<=K_agg(n)<=C(n). Taking suprema proves equivalent finiteness, not a finite universal constant. The exact first unpaid line is sup_n K_sel(n)<infinity (or the separate aggregate payment B<=KI).

**Zero-erasure transport.** Each packet entropy is nonnegative and vanishes precisely when u*v=0. Thus B_i=0 iff all supported pairs have at most one endpoint. Projection removing i is injective on the supported spectrum. Both children have the same squared coefficients s_T, hence H(child)=H(f), JS_i=0 and I(child)=I-I_i. For n>=2 a two-variable AND sign has H/I=2, so a maximizing nonconstant f has H>0. If relevant i had B_i=0, a child with I>0 would have strictly larger H/I in the same at-most-n domain. A child with I=0 would have H=0, contradicting H(child)=H(f)>0. Thus every relevant coordinate of a maximizer has positive erasure. This proves the literal lead statement without a finite census.

**Exact five-bit cutoff and scope.** Let g=-1 exactly at integer vertices 5,12,13. Independently constructing its 32 signs and applying integer FWHT gives the Walsh vector in out/FIVEBIT_EXACT.json. Parseval is sum W_S^2=1024. Physical edge counts independently match spectral influences: I_i=3/16 at i=1,2,4 and 1/16 at i=0,3. For each coordinate,

    1024 B_i=sum_T [(a+b)log2(a+b)-a log2 a-b log2 b],
    a=W_T^2, b=W_(T+i)^2.

Factoring these integers gives exactly the two expressions in B17-KB5-CUTOFF@1. At i=1,2,4 the ratio is q=16/3-(3/8)log2 3-(169/24)log2 13+(89/24)log2 89. At i=0,3 it is 16-(63/8)log2 3+15log2 5-(169/8)log2 13+(85/8)log2 17. Rational logarithm enclosures certify that the latter is larger and

    q in [2.695887520153100810486497, 2.695887520153100810486498],
    q-107/40 in [0.020887520153100810486497, 0.020887520153100810486498].

The four-variable constant is in [2.672488983973203053133973, 2.672488983973203053133974], strictly below 107/40. Its finite optimum premise is imported from A082 Theorem 1 plus the recorded B18 HOLDS review. Therefore K_sel(5)>K_sel(4). This is a checked lower bound, not an upper bound or exhaustive K_sel(5) computation. A082's original <=4-variable theorem and affine4/disjoint-product bridge remain intact. The witness has five relevant variables, and only their unsupported universal extension is refuted. Its aggregate B/I is [2.861152584249921501929567, 2.861152584249921501929568], further illustrating the two metrics' distinction. Output negation, translation and two irrelevant dummy bits preserve the five relevant ratios in the second fixed fixture.

**Pinned B09 application.** The primary node is: for finite unitary U and unit vector a, with c=max|U_kj|, H_nat(|a|^2)+H_nat(|Ua|^2)>=-2ln c. Divide by ln2 for bits. For a nonzero residual fiber T use q_T=sum_R fhat(R union T)^2, a_R=fhat(R union T)/sqrt(q_T) and b_z=sqrt(P_A(z))*fhat_z(T)/sqrt(q_T). Parseval makes both normalized. Product-basis evaluation gives b=U_A a, U_A=tensor_i [[sqrt(1-p_i),-sqrt(p_i)],[sqrt(p_i),sqrt(1-p_i)]], so c_A=product_i sqrt(max(p_i,1-p_i)). The two squared laws are the spectral conditional law and physical posterior Z|T. Average the primary entropy bound with weights q_T to obtain M_A+H(Z|T)>=-sum_i log2 max(p_i,1-p_i). Boolean child Parseval makes the Z marginal exactly the physical product law; hence H(Z)=sum_i h2(p_i) and J_A=H(Z)-H(Z|T)>=0. Subtracting gives exactly J_A<=M_A+Delta_A, with L_A=M_A+J_A. Empty A has the trivial identity experiment. The first unsupported universal implication is nonreused tree payment of conditional spectral costs, tax and terminal entropy, not the now-pinned uncertainty theorem. Necessity/optimality of the displayed tax and general substitution additivity are excluded.

# A082: sharp finite selectable-erasure certificate

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every nonconstant Boolean sign function on at most four uniform physical variables, certify a relevant coordinate i with B_i=H(S_i|S_-i)<=Kstar I_i, where P(S)=fhat(S)^2 and I_i is physical influence. Entropy is in bits. The optimal finite-domain constant is exactly Kstar=4+(5/2)log2(5)-(9/2)log2(3), approximately 2.672488983973, and Kstar<107/40. The separate intended application is the hereditary class whose Fourier support has affine F2 dimension at most four, and disjoint products of those factors, in any finite ambient dimension. This is existential selection, not an assertion that every coordinate satisfies the bound or that aggregate B<=Kstar I. Full FEI remains OPEN.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The complete proof is EXHAUSTIVENESS_LEMMA.tex, Theorems 1 and 2. With integer Walsh coefficients W=N*fhat, squared coefficients q=W^2 and D_i=sum_{S containing i}q_S, define E_i as the sum over adjacent packets of (u+v)log2(u+v)-u log2(u)-v log2(v), where u,v are squared integer Walsh coefficients. Then E_i=N^2 B_i and D_i=N^2 I_i. The inequality is exactly

2 E_i - 8 D_i log2(2) + 9 D_i log2(3) - 5 D_i log2(5) <= 0.

Factor all integers into primes and accumulate integer exponents c_p. The logarithmic sign equals the sign of the exact integer comparison product_{c_p>0} p^c_p versus product_{c_p<0} p^(-c_p). The exhaustive checker covers every table modulo output negation and chooses a passing relevant coordinate. The three-variable point defect has empty mass 9/16 and seven other masses 1/16, I_i=1/4 and B_i=(10/16)h2(1/10)+6/16 at every coordinate. This gives Kstar exactly and proves sharpness without numerical optimization. The rational simplification Kstar<107/40 is the integer inequality 2^53*5^100<3^180.

The distinct exhaustiveness theorem uses physical coordinate geometry: in support c+V, a relevant e_i outside V gives no adjacent supported pair, hence B_i=JS_i=0. If every relevant e_i lies in V, their independence leaves at most four relevant physical variables. Coordinate restriction projects c+V and cannot increase affine dimension. Disjoint products have independent factor spectra and preserve the selection payment. The exact entropy decrement B_i+JS_i and influence conservation therefore yield H<=(Kstar+2/ln2)I by physical induction with zero-entropy constant leaves, including all terminal entropy. The bound is approximately 5.557879065751; its improvement over A026's certificate is exactly 7/2-Kstar, approximately 0.827511016027. The same finite constant is optimal for the selectable-erasure property of the whole stated structural class because it contains the sharp three-variable witness. No optimality claim is made for the resulting H/I constant.

The first unsupported universal line is that an unrestricted residual can be transported to these hereditary leaves by a proper physical block/stopping rule with a uniformly influence-paid cumulative entropy cost. No such rule is supplied. General affine changes are not physical-coordinate reductions. A zero-erasure parity lift is not an easy terminal merely because B vanishes. Sparse activated bent defects can have expensive coordinates; this theorem permits selecting a different coordinate and does not contradict them. Overlapping products are excluded; independent product tensorization is the only product step used. Thus C018's aggregate universal inequality has not been proved, and this class result does not establish or disprove full FEI.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

For positive rationals c<=d, inversion reverses order. Multiply c<=d by positive 1/c to get 1<=(1/c)d, then cancel the positive factor d to conclude 1/d<=1/c. Multiplication by a nonnegative x preserves this order, hence x/d<=x/c. Multiplication by negative x reverses it; the proof obtains this by negating x and then negating the resulting quotient inequality. For each fixed positive y, a<=x<=b implies a/y<=x/y<=b/y.

Split on the sign of x. If x>=0, then a/d<=x/d<=x/y<=x/c<=b/c. If x<=0, then a/c<=x/c<=x/y<=x/d<=b/d. The minimum of all four corners is below either lower bound and the maximum is above either upper bound. `positive_denominator_enclosure` checks the two-case proof; `four_corner_enclosure` instantiates its bounds with nested min/max. Closed regression propositions check that the old rule reverses endpoints on [-1,-1]/[1,2], and that the correct corners for [-2,-1]/[1,2] give [-2,-1/2].

The successful formal artifact is code/RationalDivision.lean. Earlier Mathlib ordered-field drafts are retained as unchecked attempts. No theorem relies on them. The rational statement has no unproved line when the saved compiler output is PASS and its axiom list excludes sorryAx. The real-number extension is outside the formal scope: the same ordered-field argument applies over the reals, or one passes from rational approximants inside the closed intervals using quotient continuity on y>=c>0. Applying this to ln(x)/ln(2) still requires independently proved real logarithm enclosures and strictly positive ln(2) bounds.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Kernel core: O_n and E_n are positive products. At n=1, E_1=2=2O_1. If E_n<=2nO_n for n>=1, then

    E_(n+1)=(2n+2)E_n <= (2n+2)(2n)O_n
       <= (2n+2)(2n+1)O_n = 2(n+1)O_(n+1).

Natural arithmetic proves this by induction. Exact product identities, also checked by induction, give (2n)!=O_nE_n and E_n=2^n n!. Hence on paper O_n/E_n=(2n)!/[4^n(n!)^2]=binom(2n,n)/4^n. Rational order cancellation of positive E_n and 2n gives 1/(2n)<=O_n/E_n. Multiplication by positive 1/(2n-1) gives the coefficient inequality. Multiplication by nonnegative t^n and induction on the number of terms gives the formal theorem all_partial_sums. No finite maximum index is used.

Analytic extension, outside Lean: F(0)=F'(0)=0 and F''(z)=1/(1-z^2). For |z|<1 the geometric series for this second derivative converges uniformly on every compact subinterval. Integrating twice yields

    F(z)=sum_(n>=1) z^(2n)/[2n(2n-1)].

For completeness the required binomial expansion can be reconstructed without an external theorem: a_0=1 and a_(n+1)=a_n(2n+1)/(2n+2), so 0<a_n<=1. The series A(t)=sum_(n>=0)a_n t^n and its termwise derivative converge on compact subsets of |t|<1, dominated by geometric and differentiated geometric series. The recurrence gives 2(1-t)A'(t)=A(t) and A(0)=1. Differentiating sqrt(1-t)A(t) shows it is constant, hence A(t)=(1-t)^(-1/2). Integrating its half multiple from 0 to t and using a_n=a_(n-1)(2n-1)/(2n) yields

    1-sqrt(1-t)=sum_(n>=1) a_n t^n/(2n-1).

Set t=z^2. For each finite N the polynomial inequality extends from rational t>=0 to real t>=0 by density and continuity. For 0<=t<1 both partial sums converge to the displayed functions, so their order passes to the limit. At z=+-1, continuity of x ln x at 0 and of the square root gives the endpoint inequality by approaching from the interior. Evenness covers negative z. The first step outside the kernel certificate is the real-series identity for F; the binomial expansion, real-density transfer and endpoint limits are likewise not formalized here. They are complete analytic arguments on the page, not hidden formal premises.

Packet application, also outside Lean: for u=fhat(T), v=fhat(T+i), write s=u^2+v^2. When s>0, z=2uv/s lies in [-1,1]; at s=0 the packet is zero. The physical child/spectral joint masses are s(1+z)/2 and s(1-z)/2, and Boolean normalization makes the physical child marginal fair. Its contribution to JS_i is sF(z)/ln2. Since

    sG(z)=s-|u^2-v^2|=2min(u^2,v^2),

the scalar inequality gives JS_i<=(2/ln2)sum_T min(u^2,v^2)<=(2/ln2)min(I_i,1-I_i). This does not bound B_i=H(S_i|S_-i), and therefore does not finish C018 or C019.

# exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Formal statement: for all rational p0,p1,a,b, with a,b>=0 and p0+p1=(a+b)/2, define k(a,b)=a/(a+b) on positive rows, with k=1/2 on the zero row. Then (p0+p1)k(a,b)=a/2 and k(a,b)+k(b,a)=1. Nonnegativity makes zero sum imply a=b=0. The certificate covers normalization and unreachable-row handling, not entropy or a universal reference payment.

Exact Boolean family: for every integer r>=1, let f_r(x)=product_(j=0..r-1)(1-2x_(2j)x_(2j+1)), x_i in {0,1}, with independent uniform bits. Query each block's first bit; query its second bit only when the first is 1; process blocks in fixed order. This is an essential computing tree, never repeating a coordinate. Let Pi be its stopped transcript, S0 its coherently retained root Fourier label, and E its terminal residual label. Every leaf is constant, so E=empty. Put Y=f_r(X), C_root=H(Pi|Y)-H(Pi|S0) and C_reset=H(Pi|Y)-H(Pi|E). The exact claim is

    H(Pi|S0)=r/2, H(Pi|E)=3r/2,
    C_root=r-h2((1-2^-r)/2), C_reset=-h2((1-2^-r)/2),
    C_root-C_reset=r, sum_v w_v J_v=r, H(f_r)=2r, I(f_r)=r.

Thus substituting terminal-label conditioning into the root-conditioned conversion identity is not free. A universal influence payment for this omitted reset difference must have coefficient at least 1, or at least 1/2 for the half bank. This is not an FEI counterexample: H/I=2 for every r.

# complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Local coherent transport: if parent coefficients are u=(Ghat(T)+Hhat(T))/2, v=(Ghat(T)-Hhat(T))/2, then u^2+v^2=(Ghat(T)^2+Hhat(T)^2)/2. Set a=Ghat(T)^2,b=Hhat(T)^2. Conditional on the erased row T, the physical branch kernel is a/(a+b) or b/(a+b). Multiplying by row mass (a+b)/2 gives a/2 or b/2 exactly. This retains the original full label and only uses its current projection to select the branch. For a+b=0 the row mass is zero, so the normalized fallback adds no true mass. Summing over T and applying the child's Parseval identity yields fair physical branches. The local rational statements are checked in Lean; the Fourier identity and tree interpretation are outside its scope.

One-block witness: f=[1,1,1,-1] indexed by x0+2x1 has Walsh amplitudes (1/2,1/2,1/2,-1/2) and four root masses 1/4. Its transcript alphabet is 0 (x0=0), 10 (x0=1,x1=0), 11 (x0=1,x1=1). Under the actual coherent coupling, the only nonzero joint masses are

| S0 | Pi | probability |
|---|---|---:|
| 0 | 0 | 1/4 |
| 1 | 0 | 1/4 |
| 2 | 10 | 1/8 |
| 2 | 11 | 1/8 |
| 3 | 10 | 1/8 |
| 3 | 11 | 1/8 |

The transcript law is (1/2,1/4,1/4), with H(Pi)=3/2. Root entropy is 2 and joint entropy is 5/2, so H(Pi|S0)=1/2 and I(Pi;S0)=1. Terminal E is empty, giving H(Pi|E)=3/2. Output entropy is h2(1/4)=2-(3/4)log2(3), and the transcript determines output. Consequently C_root=-1+(3/4)log2(3)>0, while C_reset=-2+(3/4)log2(3)<0. The strict signs are integer comparisons 27>16 and 27<256. The reset changes both magnitude and sign. Dimension two is minimal: on zero or one bit every Boolean sign is constant or a literal, with deterministic root spectrum, hence no lost root/transcript mutual information.

All-r bridge: Fourier coefficients of disjoint products factor and root spectra tensor. At each block, preceding evaluated signs multiply the remaining function by a sign; this changes no squared branch coefficient. Unqueried other blocks contribute the same tensor factor to numerator and row denominator, and it cancels from the coherent branch kernel. Therefore the joint root-label/transcript law is the tensor product of the displayed six-entry block law, for every r, not just the tested range. Entropy additivity gives H(Pi)=3r/2, H(S0)=2r, H(S0,Pi)=5r/2. Each factor has mean 1/2, so E Y=2^-r and P(Y=-1)=(1-2^-r)/2. The transcript determines Y. This proves all displayed bank formulas.

Each first-bit query has J=1 and each reached second-bit query has J=0; the weighted total is r. Every physical coordinate in an AND block is pivotal with probability 1/2, so each block has total influence 1 and the product has influence r. All terminal Fourier entropies are zero because the tree computes the function. Thus the example respects the same consumed influence R=r and the existing A092 upper bound; it refutes only a proposed free substitution of E for S0. It says nothing about a complete witness that additionally retains history or extra side information: its conditional-bank/reset correction must be recomputed explicitly.

First unsupported universal line: a prescribed normalized witness/reference channel funds its particular conditional bank, reset correction, erasure residual and terminal core entropy from a remaining exclusive influence reserve. The kernel check and this obstruction neither supply that reference nor justify spending the same r units again elsewhere.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For independent X~Bernoulli(p), Y~Bernoulli(q), g(X,Y)=(-1)^(XY), and physical restriction A={X}, determine the sign of F(p,q)=J_A-M_A for every (p,q) in (0,1)^2. The orthonormal characters are (X-p)/sqrt(p(1-p)) and (Y-q)/sqrt(q(1-q)). All entropies are in bits. J_A is mutual information between the physical X child and its remaining spectral label; M_A is conditional entropy of the spectral X label given the spectral Y label. Boundary values below mean continuous limits of this experiment.

**Resolved scope:** F>0 throughout the real rectangle [1/10,3/20] x [1/5,3/10]; a separate certified cover gives 94 negative boxes in [1/16,15/16]^2. Boundary identities and pointwise boundary sign reductions are proved below. **The requested full-square sign map is OPEN.** Its compact cover retains 902 unresolved boxes. No assertion that a grid covers all real biases is made.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Put r=4q(1-q), t=pr. Expanding 1-2XY by centered Bernoulli characters gives the signed coefficients

    1-2pq, -2q sqrt(p(1-p)), -2p sqrt(q(1-q)),
    -2 sqrt(p(1-p)q(1-q)).

Thus the squared masses in the order empty,X,Y,XY are

    a=(1-2pq)^2, b=4p(1-p)q^2, c=p^2 r, d=p(1-p)r.

Their sum is 1 and c+d=t. The physical X=0 child is constant; the X=1 child is 1-2Y, with nonempty spectral mass r. Consequently

    J_A=h(t)-p h(r),
    M_A=H(a,b,c,d)-h(t),
    F=2h(t)-p h(r)-H(a,b,c,d).                 (1)

Here h is binary entropy and 0 log 0=0. The useful alternative, avoiding the four-atom entropy, is

    M_A=(1-t)h(b/(1-t))+t h(p),
    F=h(t)-p h(r)-(1-t)h(b/(1-t))-t h(p).     (2)

At the isolated point t=1 use the continuous interpretation. This occurs only at p=1,q=1/2 on the closed square.

### Exact boundaries and the internal zero line

Direct substitution in (1) yields

    F(0,q)=F(1,q)=F(p,0)=0,
    F(p,1)=-h(4p(1-p)),
    F(p,1/2)=0.                              (3)

The entire q=1/2 line is therefore zero, not merely the rational sample points. On that line the four masses are ((1-p)^2,p(1-p),p^2,p(1-p)), so their entropy is 2h(p), whereas t=p and r=1.

For a fixed p in (0,1), differentiation at q=1/2 is legitimate: the potentially singular term h(r) has increment O(u^2 log(1/|u|)) where u=q-1/2, and all four parent masses there are positive. Since t_q=r_q=0 at q=1/2 and a_q=-4p(1-p), b_q=4p(1-p), c_q=d_q=0,

    F(p,1/2+u)=-4p(1-p) log2((1-p)/p) u
                +O_p(u^2 log(1/|u|)).        (4)

For fixed p<1/2, F is positive just below that line and negative just above it. For fixed p>1/2 the signs reverse. These neighborhoods depend on p. No assertion of a uniform strip through p=1/2 or p=1 is justified by (4).

The four outer edges also have analytic reductions. The elementary natural-log expansion is

    h(z)=z log2(1/z)+z/ln(2)+E(z),
    |E(z)| <= z^2/[2 ln(2)(1-z)]  (0<=z<1). (5)

Indeed, -(1-z)ln(1-z)-z=-sum_{k>=2} z^k/[k(k-1)], with absolute sum at most z^2/[2(1-z)]. Using (5) on vanishing atoms and ordinary Taylor estimates on positive atoms in (1) proves the following pointwise limits:

    lim_{p->0} F(p,q)/(p log2(1/p))=4q(1-2q)
        for every fixed 0<q<1;
    lim_{p->1} F(p,q)/((1-p)log2(1/(1-p)))=-4q
        for every fixed 0<q<1, q!=1/2;
    lim_{q->0} F(p,q)/q=4p(1-p)log2((1-p)/p)
        for every fixed 0<p<1.               (6)

For clarity, in the first limit the atom entropy coefficient is b+d=4pq+O(p^2); twice h(t) contributes 2pr, and p h(r) has no p log p term. In the second limit the newly vanishing atoms b,d have total mass 4q(1-p)+O((1-p)^2); J has no (1-p)log(1-p) term when r<1. In the third limit, b=O(q^2), c+d=4pq+O(q^2), and H=h(4pq)+4pq h(p)+O_p(q^2 log(1/q)); this gives the stated coefficient.

At p=1/2, the third coefficient vanishes, but the exact identity H=2h(q) gives

    F(1/2,q)=2h(2q(1-q))-(1/2)h(4q(1-q))-2h(q)
            =-2q^2 log2(1/q)+O(q^2)          (q->0). (7)

This is negative sufficiently close to q=0. It is symmetric under q->1-q, proving the same near q=1. For every other fixed interior p, F(p,1)<0 by (3) and continuity. Thus all four edges have pointwise analytic sign reductions, including their exceptional fixed-bias cases. Joint corner scaling and transition curves are not settled by these pointwise reductions.

The extra zeros can already be proved to exist, without assigning them shapes. For every fixed p>1/2, (4) gives F>0 just above q=1/2 while (3) gives F<0 near q=1. The intermediate value theorem supplies at least one extra zero in (1/2,1). Moreover code/topology.py certifies F(49/100,1/4)<-11/100. At p=49/100, (6) gives a positive neighborhood of q=0 and (4) a positive neighborhood just below q=1/2. There are therefore at least two extra zeros in (0,1/2), one on each side of q=1/4. The fixed point and exact log enclosure are saved in out/ZERO_TOPOLOGY.json. This refutes a proposed sign map containing only q=1/2, and shows why a universal monotonicity claim in q cannot be used.

### Interval certificate: all points of each box are covered

The checker uses only fractions for signs. For x>0, multiply x by a power of 2 to place it in [1,2], and set z=(x-1)/(x+1). For N=20,

    ln x = 2 sum_{j=0}^{N-1} z^(2j+1)/(2j+1) + R_N,
    |R_N| <= 2|z|^(2N+1)/[(2N+1)(1-z^2)].

The same series encloses ln 2. The quotient is enclosed by the minimum and maximum of all four endpoint quotients; this handles negative logarithms correctly. Bounds are rounded outward to multiples of 2^-48 using integer floor/ceiling. No binary64 values participate in a sign decision.

Interval addition, multiplication and squaring enclose the exact masses. Intersecting a mass interval with [0,1] is valid since these are genuine probability atoms. The interval for r uses concavity of 4q(1-q). For phi(x)=-x log2 x with a positive lower endpoint, monotonicity of log and interval multiplication suffice. If a mass interval starts at zero and has upper endpoint <=1/4, phi is increasing there (ln 4>1), so [0,phi(upper)] encloses it. Otherwise [0,1] is a valid crude bound. Binary entropy intervals use its concavity, with maximum 1 if the interval crosses 1/2. Combining these operations in (1) encloses F on the entire box.

The positive rectangle splits into 64 final boxes, each with rational F lower endpoint >0. Each split bisects one coordinate and retains both children, so the union of the leaves is the complete initial rectangle, including its boundary. The compact cover uses the same rule; its sign-negative leaves are rigorous, while unresolved leaves are retained.

### Finite-to-continuum completeness lemma, with its limitation

For a compact K contained in (0,1)^2 with a known positive separation min_K |F|>0, adaptive rational boxes and successively increased N and output bits produce a finite sign certificate on K. Proof: phi and h are continuous including their zero endpoints; their interval enclosures shrink with the argument intervals and arithmetic errors. Every point with nonzero F has a sufficiently small rational neighborhood with the same certified strict sign. Compactness gives a finite subcover. This is a rigorous continuum bridge, but the separation assumption cannot be silently applied to this whole square: it contains the exact zero line and additional sign transitions. Fixed N=20,48 bits alone cannot certify arbitrarily tiny strict margins.

**Exact first unsupported line:** that the unknown additional zero curves have a specified count/shape, or that all 902 unresolved compact boxes can be assigned a sign by the fixed-bias boundary reductions. Neither has been proved. In particular an exact description of the complete failure region {F>0} beyond the certified open/closed rectangles remains open.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For every independent Bernoulli input pair (p,q) in (0,1)^2, consider the genuine Boolean gate g(X,Y)=(-1)^(XY), the normalized product Fourier basis, and actual physical block A={X}. Classify F(p,q)=J_A-M_A, where J_A is physical-child/remaining-spectral-label mutual information and M_A is conditional spectral-label entropy. Units are bits. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

The complete derivation is in PROOF.md. It is source-complete and does not rely on the grid or on the universal B09 uncertainty theorem. Its decisive reduction is

    theta=arcsin(sqrt(p)),
    alpha=atan(sqrt(p/(1-p))(1-2q)),
    psi(x)=h(sin^2 x), w=1-4pq(1-q)>0,
    F(p,q)=w[psi(theta)-psi(alpha)-psi(theta-alpha)].

This follows from the actual conditional physical and spectral laws in the remaining-label-zero sector. The label-one contribution cancels exactly. On (0,pi/2), differentiation and y=cot(2x) give

    (ln 2)psi''''(x)=16[2+y^2-y asinh(y)/sqrt(1+y^2)]>=32.

The elementary bound |asinh(y)|<=|y| proves the sign. For G_s(x)=psi(x)+psi(s-x)-psi(s), its derivative is strictly concave on (0,s/2), since G_s'''=psi'''(x)-psi'''(s-x)<0. This limits G_s to a single valley on that half-interval. A unique transition s_* is determined by 2psi(s_*/2)=psi(s_*); existence and uniqueness follow by convexity of psi before its inflection tau and strict convexity of K(s)=2psi(s/2)-psi(s) for s>=tau. Put p_*=sin^2(s_*).

For s<s_*, G_s<0 throughout the interior. At s=s_* its only interior zero is the midpoint tangency. For s_*<s<pi/4 it has exactly two simple reflected roots a(s),s-a(s), with a(s) uniquely defined in (0,s/2); G_s is positive between them and negative outside. For s>=pi/4 it is strictly positive throughout the interior. Reflections of psi convert every original bias pair to exactly one of these cases, including the region beyond its cancellation zero.

The exact full map in PROOF.md can be summarized as follows. The line q=1/2 is always zero. For p>1/2 there is the additional exact hyperbola q=1/(2p). Below q=1/2: p<p_* is entirely positive; p=p_* has one additional zero at q=1/[2(1+sqrt(1-p))]; p_*<p<1/2 has a negative interval between two positive intervals; p>=1/2 is entirely negative. Above q=1/2: p<=1/2 is entirely negative. For p>1/2 the region beyond q=1/(2p) is entirely negative; within the wedge (1/2,1/(2p)), apply the same scalar classification with effective bias 1-p. Thus p>1-p_* gives an entirely positive wedge; at p=1-p_* its midpoint zero is q=1/(2sqrt(p)); for 1/2<p<1-p_* there is a negative interval between two positive intervals.

The distinct interior q-root count, for each fixed p, is:

| Bias p | Number of q zeros |
|---|---:|
| (0,p_*) | 1 |
| p_* | 2 |
| (p_*,1/2) | 3 |
| 1/2 | 1 |
| (1/2,1-p_*) | 4 |
| 1-p_* | 3 |
| (1-p_*,1) | 2 |

The extra root branches are smooth on their open p ranges by the implicit function theorem; their definition as unique scalar roots is exact. No closed elementary formula for a(s) is assumed. Outer-edge identities are F(0,q)=F(1,q)=F(p,0)=0 and F(p,1)=-h(4p(1-p)). These, and the full interior classification, also cover arbitrary joint corner approaches; pointwise asymptotics are no longer being used as a substitute for a continuum theorem.

As a downstream consequence, both physical singleton restrictions have J_A>M_A throughout the real square 0<p,q<p_* (apply the map twice after exchanging the inputs). Hence even existential singleton selection cannot universally restore a tax-free biased restriction helper.

**First unsupported line at this target:** none identified in the derivation; independent review is required and has not occurred. The next universal unsupported line is a nonreusing influence/deficit payment for the necessary biased correction and conditional spectral entropy in a physical stopping tree. This stage does not supply that line.

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

For a Boolean core g on ANY uniform n-cube with minority probability p_min in [1/16,1/2], compose six disjoint identical oriented copies F=h(g1,...,g1). The target is a single common orientation preserving the true physical-coordinate erasure ratio B_i/Inf_i for EVERY relevant coordinate, over ALL real parameter values in that bias band. B_i=H(S_i|S_-i); entropies are bits; all physical variables and the entire terminal H(g) are retained.

**Result:** the continuum admission theorem is proved at author level. Orient g0 to its minority output probability p_min; use g1=g0 for p_min<=7/32 and g1=-g0 otherwise. Then B_(j,i)(F)/Inf_(j,i)(F)>=B_i(g)/Inf_i(g) for every relevant i,j, hence E(F)>=E(g). No high-E assumption, dimension cutoff or singleton-granularity lower bound is needed WITHIN this band. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

PROOF.md is a complete derivation. The signed coordinate identity is reconstructed from actual conditional outer sections and disjoint-block Fourier expansion:

    B_(j,i)(F)=q B_i(g)+R_m(beta_i^2),
    Inf_(j,i)(F)=q Inf_i(g),
    q=2p(1-p)^3(2-p), m=1-2p,
    R_m(t)=sum_T phi(U_T,tD_T)-q phi(m^2,t),
    phi(x,y)=(x+y)log2(x+y)-xlog2 x-ylog2 y.

Here p is the CHOSEN oriented output bias, U_T=(a_T+m d_T)^2 and D_T=d_T^2; recentering is performed before squaring. All six outer coordinates have the same reserve because the gate's matching-pair automorphisms are transitive. One output sign applies simultaneously to every core coordinate and all six blocks.

The new finite-to-continuum mechanism is an exact boundary lemma. For positive packets set c=m^2, lambda_T=D_T/q and u_T=U_T/D_T. Define A=sum D_T log2(u_T/c). The weighted geometric mean G(t)=product(t+u_T)^lambda_T satisfies G'(t)>=1 by weighted AM-GM. Thus A>=0 implies G(0)>=c, then G(t)>=t+c and R_m'(t)=q log2(G(t)/(t+c))>=0 for every t>0. Since R_m(0)=0, the slope test proves the entire t half-line, not only the attainable finite six-bit signatures. Conversely R_m(t)/t->A, so a negative slope diagnoses failure near t=0.

Direct uniform-Walsh polynomial expansion gives 12 exact packet types and FOUR U/D ratios; the formulas are in PROOF.md and out/SYMBOLIC_PACKETS.json. An outward rational-log interval certificate proves A>0 on m in [9/16,7/8] with 9 closed adjacent intervals and on m in [-9/16,-1/2] with 4. Their minimum certified lower bounds are respectively

    47949270588870825/2305843009213693952 >0,
    1334759120530679/9223372036854775808 >0.

Coverage is checked by exact endpoint equality. For the remaining complemented means [-1/2,0], PROOF.md proves every ratio U/D>=m^2 analytically using elementary polynomial bounds, with a strict positive-weight packet. At m=0 the subtraction term vanishes and phi is positive directly. At t=0 every reserve is exactly zero; there is no unclassified singular boundary. These arguments cover all real means in the stated band and every t>=0. Actual singleton t<=4p_min^2 is therefore included automatically, irrespective of core dimension.

The full terminal identities are kept:

    H(F)=6q H(g)+C_p, C_p=H_p(h)-6q h2(1-m^2),
    I(F)=6q I(g).

Neither C_p nor a reference/reset/stopping bill is dropped or claimed paid. This closes A072's dimensional gap in its SAFE BIAS BAND only; its finite 23-orbit rare-core guard is not used to exclude rare arbitrary-dimensional cores.

The analytic low-bias reductions show why the residual cannot be solved by a larger grid: for original p_min->0, the normalized boundary slope is 2log2(p_min)+6+o(1) in the minority orientation and 2log2(p_min)+2+o(1) in the complemented orientation. Both are negative eventually. These follow from the four rational packet ratios/weights and imply negative reserves near t=0 in both orientations. They concern reserve signs and do not exhibit an actual high-E core in that sector.

**First unsupported universal line:** a dimension-independent exclusion or owned signed payment for high-E cores with p_min<1/16. Even after that, an iteration-closed bias/orientation policy and a terminal-inclusive price for C_p are needed before a universal C018/C019 conclusion. Composition output bias can leave the certified band; nothing here authorizes indefinite bootstrap or unbounded E or H/I.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### A071: HOLDS on its complete finite domain

Our transform acts on a value pair u,v in coordinate bias p by replacing it with ((1-p)u+pv,v-u). Iterating coordinates produces coefficients a_S in the unnormalized basis product_{i in S}(x_i-p_i). The squared orthonormal mass is a_S^2 product_{i in S}p_i(1-p_i). This representation is different from A071's direct integer weighted-sum transform. Parseval and physical-mixture identities are checked exactly for every tested parent and block; each child comes from its actual restricted truth table. Therefore its tested defect is exactly H(parent)-2H(residual marginal)+E_Z H(child)+Delta_A.

The symmetry coverage is valid. Output complement negates all Fourier coefficients. Flipping input i and replacing p_i by 1-p_i negates the i-th centered character, preserves every squared mass, and bijects physical children and their probabilities. Reflect all biases into {1/8,1/4,1/2}, then complement the resulting table to make f(000)=+1. We explicitly map all 32,000 raw table/bias pairs onto all 3,456 representative table/bias pairs; retaining six masks covers 192,000 raw cases with 20,736 representatives. Multiplicities are 16 for 1,024 pairs, 8 for 1,536 pairs, 4 for 768 pairs, and 2 for 128 pairs. No coordinate permutation or biased-character multiplication is used.

For x>0 write x=2^k r with 1<=r<2. Set z=(r-1)/(r+1). The logarithm lower sum is 2 sum_{j=0}^{N-1} z^{2j+1}/(2j+1); the positive tail is at most 2z^{2N+1}/[(2N+1)(1-z^2)]. Our grid checker uses N=28 and floors each scaled term at 2^96. Add N units for the discarded fractional parts and a ceiling of the tail for an upper endpoint. Divide the NONNEGATIVE ln(r) interval by the POSITIVE ln2 interval, then add exact integer k. This avoids signed interval division altogether. Every subsequent signed multiplication swaps endpoints when necessary. Powers of two are exact. Zero masses are omitted from entropy, never passed to log.

A071's own signed division was also inspected: it reverses the ln2 endpoints for negative k and takes all four corner quotients. Its entropy coefficient -mass reverses endpoints. Thus the previously repaired B09 signed-division flaw is not present in this source.

Independent exact result: 19,632 strict positives, 1,104 exact zeros. Of the zeros, 504 are immediate exact interval equalities and 600 require symbolic cancellation. For each latter case we write the defect as sum c_x log2(x), factor positive rational x into primes, and verify every resulting prime coefficient is zero using Fraction arithmetic. Their complete parameter set matches A071's saved 600 zero certificates. The minimum-positive case agrees: code 4, biases (1/8,1/8,1/2), mask 4, with a certified lower bound greater than 5/2048 bits. No sign or equality uses floating arithmetic.

Empty blocks have zero defect. Full blocks have J=0 and M+Delta>=0. Dummy-coordinate embeddings cover lower-dimensional grid cases. Biases 0 and 1 are excluded from the interior orthonormal basis; their proper interpretation is contraction, not division by zero. The source does not claim them as part of its numerical grid.

First unsupported extrapolation: 'these 20,736 representatives imply all real product biases and all dimensions.' No such reduction is supplied or claimed. The universal B09 theorem requires its separate analytic proof; the finite certificate alone cannot establish it. Largest directly enumerated dimension is exactly three.

### A042: HOLDS at the fixed-table endpoint interface; finite intervals verified

Independent derivation: integrate selected coordinates in the centered product basis. With w_S=product_{i in S}4p_i(1-p_i), the mixed-difference coefficient c_S=2^{-|S|} E_{outside S} Delta_S f is a polynomial independent of the selected probabilities and bounded in magnitude by one. Consequently P(S)=w_S c_S^2 and E=sum_{S!=empty}w_S c_S^2 ln(1/c_S^2). Deficit is sum_{|S|>=2}(|S|-1)w_S c_S^2. The function t ln(1/t) extends continuously at zero. Terms meeting a deterministic coordinate vanish, and every remaining coefficient is that of the contracted table. This proves continuity of E and D, not continuity of E/D at D=0.

At a rare coordinate e with child coefficients a_T and a_T+b_T, the parent coefficients are a_T+e b_T and sqrt(e(1-e))b_T. Expanding the two degree-weighted sums gives exactly D=D0+eL-e^2Q, Q=sum_{T!=empty}b_T^2. The empty T has degree zero in the selected-coordinate deficit and is correctly excluded from Q.

For corrected entropy, the unselected T!=empty packet contributes (a_T+e b_T)^2 ln[w_T/(a_T+e b_T)^2]. The selected packet, INCLUDING T=empty, contributes e(1-e)b_T^2 ln(4w_T/b_T^2). The selector normalization cancels exactly; omitting its empty residual label would change K. Differentiating u^2 ln(w/u^2) gives 2u[ln(w/u^2)-1]. At a_T=0 the unselected term is e^2b_T^2[2ln(1/e)+ln(w_T/b_T^2)]. Summing yields E=E(g)+eK+2e^2Z ln(1/e)+O(e^2), with precisely A042's K and Z. Finitely many nonzero a_T have a positive minimum magnitude for a fixed table and fixed basis, so the Taylor remainder is valid there. That minimum need not be uniform over moving biases or dimension.

If D0=0 then the typical child is constant or a signed literal: a degree-one Boolean polynomial can have at most one nonzero slope because the pairwise degree-two coefficients in f^2 equal twice products of slopes. If also L=0, nonnegative D(e)=-e^2Q forces Q=0 and no ratio may be formed. Otherwise L>0 and division gives the stated K/(L ln2) limit. A unit spectral atom means a signed normalized product character is Boolean pointwise; varying an occupied coordinate forces equal magnitudes of its two character values, hence that coordinate is fair. Such an atom is a fair-coordinate parity; its D=|S|-1, not always zero.

For analytic paths the first nonzero orders of w and c give the stated atom expansion; zero polynomials contribute zero. Its constants belong to that fixed path/table. A042 correctly treats the exceptional weighted m=1 limit separately. We independently sum the compressed actual weighted Boolean spectrum over cardinality and heavy-coordinate status at m=2,3,4,8,16,32. All 18 certified Hplus/D, physical-TC/I and pivotal-credit/I intervals contain our independently computed tighter intervals. The original floor/ceiling directions, positive denominator checks, signed scaling and four-corner division are correct. These finite ratio checks are separate from the endpoint proof and do not constitute an all-m search or an all-arity theorem.

First unsupported extrapolation: 'a finite fixed-slice limit K/L bounds every simultaneous degenerating-bias, dimension-varying weighted threshold.' Neither continuity of E,D nor a finite collection of ratios proves this. A042 explicitly leaves it open.

### A047: HOLDS for fixed children; all 20 finite interval cases verified

Let G,H be the two normalized child coefficient vectors. Parent fibers are a_T=((1-p)G_T+pH_T)^2 and b_T=p(1-p)(H_T-G_T)^2, and q_T=(1-p)G_T^2+pH_T^2. At a positive limiting atom entropy changes by O(p); an atom p v+O(p^2) contributes v p log2(1/p)+O(p). For G_T=0, q_T and b_T have leading mass pH_T^2, whereas a_T is order p^2. For G_T!=0, only b_T can create a first-order logarithm. Therefore H(q) has coefficient Z=sum_{G_T=0}H_T^2, H(a,b) has coefficient Z+D with D=sum_{G_T!=0}(H_T-G_T)^2, and the averaged child entropy has none. Hence J-M has coefficient Z-D=2Z+2c-2, c=<G,H>=E(gh).

Because G is supported outside the zero set, Cauchy-Schwarz gives c^2<=1-Z. Thus 2Z+2c-2<=2c-2c^2=1/2-2(c-1/2)^2. The constant/quarter-defect pair attains equality; a fresh fair character shifts both spectra and makes both children balanced without changing these quantities. If supports are disjoint the residual spectrum identifies the child, so J=h2(p); each nonzero fiber has selector conditional probability p or 1-p, so M=h2(p) exactly. These proofs need only Parseval, finite entropy expansions and actual physical children.

The finite reviewer constructs the complete parent truth table and transforms it, rather than using A047's two-child fiber formulas. All 15 named-pair joint spectra agree exactly, all M,J,debt saved intervals contain our independent 44-term exact-rational enclosures, and all five unequal-bias AND debt intervals contain our independent enclosures with the stated strict signs. A047's 40-term positive range reduction correctly divides before adding the signed exponent; zero atoms and exact powers of two are handled correctly. The low-dimensional coefficient comparison in our checker is only for FAIR data cubes of dimension zero and one, not arbitrary one-bit biases.

First unsupported extrapolation: 'the fixed-pair coefficient <=1/2 implies a finite-p half-tax inequality or a remainder uniform over moving bases/dimension.' A047 expressly does not assert this. A concrete two-variable support-transition witness shows why the remainder cannot be made uniform without a new estimate: take typical child g(Y)=2Y-1, rare child h(Y)=1, with data bias q. If q!=1/2, G has both coordinates nonzero, so Z=0 and the debt coefficient is 4q-4. At q=1/2 the supports are disjoint and J-M=0 for every selector probability p. Thus lim_{q->1/2} lim_{p->0}(J-M)/(p log2(1/p))=-2, but reversing the limits gives 0. A bound |J-M-(4q-4)p log2(1/p)|<=C p uniform over q near but unequal to 1/2 is impossible: let q tend to 1/2 at fixed p, then let p tend to zero. This is an obstruction to an unsupported uniformization, not a refutation of A047's claim or of FEI.

### Explicit underflow diagnostic and tail payment

For the actual point defect f_n=1-2 1_{x=0}, put p=2^{-n}. Its empty mass is (1-2p)^2, each of 2^n-1 nonempty masses is 4p^2, and I=2np. IEEE binary64 round-to-nearest first rounds the empty AMPLITUDE to one at n=55, the directly converted empty MASS to one at n=56, and every nonempty mass to zero at n=539. Minimality within this exact family follows from binary64's predecessor gap below one (2^-53) and smallest positive subnormal (2^-1074), and is checked over the frozen scalar range n=1..540. No exponential array is allocated.

At n=539, a directly converted compressed spectrum looks like the constant function: empty mass one, all other masses zero. The exact discarded nonempty entropy is 8p(1-p)(n-1), whose ratio to exact I is 4(1-p)(n-1)/n>3.99. This is the requested explicit implementation failure, not a failure of any of the three audited rational sources. Our powers-of-two regressions include exponents +/-1075, for which rational logs remain exact.

More generally, if M atoms of total mass epsilon are dropped without renormalizing, their omitted entropy is at most epsilon log2(M/epsilon) by concavity and at least epsilon log2(1/epsilon). A support bound M<=2^n introduces dimension in the upper error. An absolute small-mass tolerance alone cannot certify H/I when I can approach zero. The point-defect diagnostic makes this ratio issue explicit.

# A085 stage 2: a false screen-error contract with surviving finite certificates

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

B17 lead v2 requests an adversarial audit of B17-KB4-CENSUS@1 and B17-KB5-CUTOFF@1, especially certlog.py's 2^-46 S floating error claim versus its 2^-40 S decision margin, exact fallback, and the 64-extremizer orbit. A071's three-bit grid was accepted in stage 1 and is not reopened. The updated board also supplies the completed A071/A082 five-bit census, so this stage audits its 40-bit integer lookup and coverage rather than rerunning it.

For Boolean witnesses, the measure is uniform, f takes signs, P(S)=fhat(S)^2, I_i=sum_{S containing i}P(S), B_i=H(S_i|S_-i), and all entropies use bits. Dimension is fixed at four, five or six; only named witnesses/orbits are generated. A generic prime-log screen test is explicitly a numerical helper test, not a Boolean FEI counterexample.

The helper's documented target is |computed sum - sum e_p log2(p)| < 2^-46 S, with S=sum |e_p| log2(p), integer coefficients |e_p|<2^50 and at most 600 terms. We refute that statement on an allowed 130-prime input in NumPy 2.4.3. We separately prove the existing 2^-40 S_computed sign decision safe on its intended matching-dimension domain of at most the first 600 primes. The source's actual four-bit comparisons have only 54 primes and satisfy even its stronger advertised bound.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### ERROR: the helper's unrestricted 600-term 2^-46 S bound

The exact integer coefficient vector is saved in out/small_failure_certificate.json. Its first coefficient, at prime 2, is 2^49; the other 129 coefficients lie in {1,...,4096}; the primes run through 733. Construct two identical rows in a Fortran-contiguous int64 array of shape (2,130). This satisfies both source assertions: max absolute coefficient <2^50 and last dimension <=600. LinLog(733) provides exactly these 130 prime constants. The multiplication result retains the strided reduction layout, and the observed source sum accumulates upward rounding errors.

Our independent rational log intervals certify

    8.0557 < computed value - exact prime-log sum < 8.0558,
    2^-46 S_exact < 8.0001.

The same strict violation holds if the advertised S is interpreted as the source-computed positive absolute sum, rather than the exact sum. Both computed hex value and complete rational endpoints are saved. The actual measured source error is approximately 8.05573538159, against a bound approximately 8.00000003236. Decimal displays are not the proof: the rational comparisons to 80557/10000, 40279/5000 and 80001/10000 are the certificate.

The construction tests all prefixes of one deterministic 600-coefficient row; 130 is its first failed prefix. A second execution uses the genuinely smaller 130-column helper and array, not a 600-column array with trailing zeros. This is the smallest tested failure in that construction, not a claim of global minimality over all floating inputs. The C-contiguous version gives a different, smaller error; the helper makes no layout restriction. The witness is a generic prime-log form, not asserted to arise from an actual Boolean spectrum.

First false source line: certlog.py, LinLog.float_screen docstring, 'integer entries <2^50 and <=600 terms ... differs ... by <2^-46*S.' No original Boolean inequality or finite constant is refuted. The returned sign in this positive witness is correct.

### HOLDS: the existing sign threshold and the actual four-bit error bound

Let u=2^-53. Each source log constant for the first 600 primes is checked against independently enclosed true log2(p), certifying relative error at most u. For matching-size integer coefficient vectors with |e|<2^50, float conversion is exact. Products and sums are normal and far from overflow in this domain. Under IEEE round-to-nearest, multiplication followed by any reduction tree of at most m-1 additions has standard elementary product-of-rounding-factors bound gamma_m=mu/(1-mu). This follows by multiplying factors (1+delta), |delta|<=u, and bounding the accumulated relative deviation by gamma_m; cancellation is handled by absolute term sums, not relative error in the result.

Writing T=sum e_p log2(p) and S=sum |e_p| log2(p), both the signed sum and the computed absolute sum obey

    |T_float-T| <= eta_m S,
    |S_float-S| <= eta_m S,
    eta_m = u+(1+u)gamma_m = (m+1)/(2^53-m).

At m=54, eta=55/(2^53-54)<2^-46, proving the advertised bound in the actual census domain, independently of contiguity or reduction order. At m=600, eta=601/(2^53-600)<2^-43. The exact rational inequality

    2^-40 (1-eta_600) > eta_600

means a source decision T_float>2^-40 S_float forces T>0; the negative case is identical. Thus weakening the advertised general error bound to 2^-43 S would fix its intended-domain contract while leaving every sign decision unchanged. No application should silently depend on pairwise summation when the API accepts other layouts. The matching-vector/prime-count and integer domain hypotheses must be stated; broadcast-mismatched arrays are outside this proved contract.

For the source's rational fallback, logarithms use a positive atanh series and a valid positive remainder; signed coefficient scaling swaps endpoints. Rounding at scale 2^320 encloses the already rigorous interval. It does NOT imply the interval width is at most 2^-320: the 90-term log remainder can dominate that rounding unit. The source correctly carries its true lower/upper endpoints and falls back to comparing products of integer prime powers if needed. Unique prime factorization proves zero iff all distinct-prime coefficients vanish. Its maximum-bigint guard raises an error rather than declaring an unresolved sign. Seven independent approximant forms exercise sign comparisons; at least three are screened as undecided and then correctly resolved by source sign_form. Exact zero is checked separately. No precision gap is found in this fallback logic.

### Four-bit comparison logic and equality-orbit scope

For N=16, W_s is an integer Walsh coefficient, q_s=W_s^2 and sum q=N^2=256. The exact prime-log numerator of B_i is sum over spectral pairs of [(a+b)log2(a+b)-a log2(a)-b log2(b)], and its influence denominator is L_i=sum_{s containing i}q_s. Entropy numerator is 2048-sum q_s log2(q_s). The decrement formula includes the correct restriction denominators and has constant term 512 at parent scale. Our direct physical restrictions confirm the reported decrement value on the attaining witness.

A floating argmin may select ANY relevant coordinate for a universal upper comparison: proving its ratio <=the target proves the minimum <=the target, even if it is not the exact argmin. For attainment, all relevant coordinates of the chosen maximizing witness must separately meet or exceed the target; the source does this. For extremizer classification, an exact zero for just the chosen coordinate alone is not enough. The missing logical step is supplied by orbit saturation: the known attaining orbit has 64 distinct tables, every one has its minimum at the target, and the certificate has exactly 64 zero selected-coordinate rows. Thus no other extremizer exists. We independently generate this orbit, then compare it AS A SET with A074's 64 masks from its separate complete census.

The 64 tables are point defects on three physical coordinates with one dummy coordinate: their minority support is an edge of the four-cube. There are 4*2^3=32 such negative supports and 32 complements. The H/I point-function orbit has 16 negative singletons and 16 complements. The aggregate B/I orbit has a three-point path in two coordinate directions, with unique degree-two center: 16*C(4,2)=96 negative supports and 96 complements. Our code generates the respective 64, 32 and 192 orbits and verifies the witness prime-log values stated by B17. For the latter two comparisons, source counts plus the known attaining orbit give the same saturation argument.

Range checks close a separate integer risk. Using v_p(m)<=8 for m<=256, mass sums and the source vector formulas bound entropy, erasure and decrement prime coefficients in magnitude by 4096, and the four-coordinate aggregate by 16384. The conservative comparison bound 2*1024*16384=33554432 is below 2^50 and int64 capacity. No overflow occurs before the helper's assertion in these actual forms. Zero masses contribute zero; irrelevant L_i=0 coordinates are omitted; the two constants are excluded. Max directly enumerated dimension remains four.

This is an independent audit of the census logic, screen, witnesses and orbit completion, not another execution of its 65,536-table job. The source's entire bundled upper census remains its original executed artifact; A074 independently supplies the selectable-erasure component. We issue a numerical-logic scope verdict, not a claim that A085 reproduced all four source maxima by a new full census.

### Five-bit cutoff, integer lookup and covering filters

For g=-1 exactly on {5,12,13}, our independent Walsh and direct character sums agree, inverse transform reconstructs every sign, and physical edge counts independently verify all influences. Coordinates {1,2,4} have I_i=3/16 and ratio

    K5=16/3-(3/8)log2(3)-(169/24)log2(13)+(89/24)log2(89).

Coordinates {0,3} have I_i=1/16 and the stated higher ratio 16-(63/8)log2(3)+15log2(5)-(169/8)log2(13)+(85/8)log2(17). Exact intervals prove these are higher and K5>107/40>K4. This independently accepts B17-KB5-CUTOFF@1 without a finite-to-universal inference.

Our six-bit reconstruction has negative assignments {0,1,2,4,8,16,32,6,17,40}. Every coordinate ratio is certified >368/125>K5. Thus even the newer five-bit maximum is already an invalid all-dimensional constant. This is a finite scope obstruction, not unbounded H/I or a disproof of FEI.

For A071's newer five-bit certificate, W/2 has integer squares of total 256. We independently enclose every one of its 289 lookup entries G(a^2,b^2), 0<=a,b<=16, and prove its stored 40-bit upper integer bounds the true packet. Zero packets are exactly zero. The seed lower integer 2964159675584/2^40 is strictly below independently certified K5. Positive-denominator quotient ceilings preserve the upper ratio bound; taking the minimum of the coordinate upper bounds is valid for existential selection.

Each lookup entry is at most 512*2^40+1, because packet entropy <=a^2+b^2<=512 even for unrealizable table entries. Sixteen packets plus quotient-ceiling adjustment are at most 9007199254741264, well below 2^63. These integers can exceed the exact-double 2^53 threshold; the source keeps them as int64, so this creates no precision failure. Only floats printed for display are used.

Coverage filters commute in the required direction: output complement makes a nonempty minority support of size <=16; coordinate permutation orders its physical influences; translation preserves that ordered influence vector and can anchor a negative point at zero; choosing the least anchored translation preserves both properties. These are covering filters, not unique orbit labels. Bit swaps in the uint32 translation code are permutation of the 32 physical assignment bits; the masks retain both swapped halves. Enumeration uses uint64 arange before the safe uint32 cast and stops at 2^32, excluding that endpoint, so the last odd mask is 2^32-1 and no wrap introduces or drops a mask. There is no affine coordinate mixing.

The 640 five-bit extrema are exactly 32*C(5,2)*2; unique center proves no double count. A071 and A082's full independent runs both report 5,778,648 representatives and the same little-endian stream hash ac010403c1e2e329262b930668c4d34bf548c862cad687ebd9210579dd0c233b. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

PROOF.md gives the complete all-dimensional argument. For the smaller sign class A of size a, internal conditional-fiber entropy and the chain rule imply b(A)>=a(N-log2 a). Strict concavity endpoint comparison shows the minimum nonzero influence is N 2^(1-N); for N>=3 equality occurs precisely at one-point defects and their complements. N=1 has its two nonconstant signs; N=2 also has four signed dictators, alongside eight point/complement signs. The N=2 exceptions are retained explicitly.

Set s_*(N)=N-1-log2 N, so 2^-s_*=N 2^(1-N). For each point defect, all nonempty Fourier masses are 4*2^-2N. Equal-mass edge counting gives

    R_geell/I = 2-4*2^-N sum_(r=0..ell-1) binom(N-1,r),
    M_geell/I = (R_geell/I)/2.

The signed-dictator exceptions contribute zero tail. For every fixed ell>=2 the endpoint suprema are therefore exactly 2 and 1, approached as N tends to infinity and never attained at finite N. An actual five-bit witness independently certifies K_E>107/40>2. Also M>=B-I/ln2 and ln2>2/3 imply K_M>47/40>1. Thus the loss-free optimal-constant transport cannot be extended to arbitrary nonnegative cutoffs. The endpoint cutoff remains nonempty in every dimension, so the obstruction is stronger than the empty-class example s(N)=N. A strict cutoff above s_* has no nonconstant functions; constants are excluded before division.

Neither audited source asserts the false extension. Their stated sublinear hypotheses survive. PROOF.md independently checks the nonmonotone tail-envelope construction, its eventual dimension bound, logarithmic influence overhead, fixed-ell requirement and extended-real supremum logic. This is a completed cutoff scope check, not a full replay of either source's finite activation checker and not an independent acceptance of the assembled FEI chain. The first unsupported universal line remains one finite bound on K_E, equivalently a retained terminal-core KL payment. No finite-dimensional computation closes that line.

ACTIVATION_DEBT_PROJECTION.md proves a directly usable universal-audit refinement. For balanced core g, F=1+A_r(g-1), p=2^-r, v=p(2-p), and any complete subprobability reference Q positive on the target support, normalize q=Q/M. Let beta=1/(2-p), gamma=q(data spectrum nonempty), and q_g be its conditional nonempty data law. Then

    vD(P_F^+||Q) = -vlog2 M + vD_Ber(beta||gamma)
                    +pD(P_g||q_g) + nonnegative control conditional KL.

The two control conditional laws are uniform over all control subsets when data is nonempty, and over nonempty control subsets when data is empty. Thus vD(P_F^+||Q)>=pD(P_g||q_g), with the exact original p coefficient. This does not lower-bound seed entropy or prove impossibility of cheap references: q_g=P_g makes that term zero. The first unsupported universal line remains construction of one admissible structural reference paying data, sector, control and normalization debt for every Boolean seed. Neither common-root cancellation nor quotient existence proves it.
