## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### Physical blocks and the conserved budget

Partition the current physical coordinates into queried U and remaining V. Write a_(R,T) for the actual Fourier coefficients with R subset U and T subset V. For a fair physical assignment z to U, the child coefficient is

    childhat_z(T)=sum_R a_(R,T) chi_R(z).

Each squared child spectrum p_z is a probability law because the child is Boolean. Partial Parseval gives q_T=E_z p_z(T)=sum_R a_(R,T)^2, the V-marginal of the parent spectrum. Therefore

    D_U=H(f)-E_z H(f_z)=H(S_U|S_V)+I(Z;T),
    I(f)=I_U(f)+E_z I(f_z).

Here Z is sampled uniformly first and T conditionally from p_Z in a one-node channel. Its posterior Z given T is generally not uniform. That posterior must not replace the physical averaging law in the second identity or in a restriction tree. For a two-control activation of a dictator, the two child empty spectral probabilities are 1 and 1/4. Conditioned on the empty packet, the constant child's probability is 4/5, while its physical probability is 1/2. The physical average child entropy is 1 bit; incorrectly using those empty-packet posterior weights gives 2/5 bit. This is an illustrative false substitution, not an error found in an audited claim.

For each original label j not yet queried, reveals of other labels preserve its conditional expected influence by edge averaging. Querying j removes precisely its current influence and replaces it by that query cost. Thus each label separately conserves expected queried cost plus terminal influence. Summing labels gives the total identity above. It does not give a pathwise decreasing wallet: activated two-bit parity has root influence 3/2 and an active child of influence 2. Summing all node influences rather than queried influences reuses budget; a full three-bit parity chain has sum of node influences 3+2+1=6 but total queried influence 3.

At each reached node the selected fresh block is measurable from the independent policy seed and observed history. Unobserved signs remain independent and fair, so the physical child probabilities are 2^(-|U|). Finite summation, including the predictable stopping indicator, gives

    H(f)=E sum D_v + E H(leaf),
    I(f)=E sum I_(U_v)(f_v) + E I(leaf).

The same tree and the same physical history weights must be used in both. This reconstructs A061-FAIR-TREE-PROCESS v1 and A062-INFLUENCE-STOPPING-INTERFACE v1, including the labelled extension. Independent randomization is handled by conditioning on its seed; averaging afterward changes neither identity.

For one fixed finite dimension, there are at most n fresh reveals even with unbounded waiting. Residual H and I are at most n, and accumulated nonnegative entropy decrements are at most n^2. Bounded convergence therefore justifies passage from finite truncation to the eventual restriction. Across dimensions these bounds are not uniform. In the abstract unbounded extension, nonnegative cumulative costs converge in L1 by monotone convergence and the initial budget. Residual influence is a nonnegative supermartingale. Fatou gives only E cost+E limiting residual <= initial influence; uniform integrability of residual influence gives equality. Entropy needs its own uniform-integrability assumption and limit identification. Those assumptions are explicitly retained by A062.

### A061 logarithmic compensation and scalar boundaries

For a coordinate use actual parent coefficients a_T,b_T, q_T=a_T^2+b_T^2, theta=b_T^2/q_T. The erasure is B=sum q h2(theta), the child-channel information is JS, and the queried influence is d=sum b_T^2. Let lambda=sum b_T^2 log(q_T/b_T^2).

The inequality -(1-theta)ln(1-theta)<=theta proves B<=lambda+d/ln2, including theta=0,1 by continuity. For z=2|ab|/q, set dnat(z)=((1+z)ln(1+z)+(1-z)ln(1-z))/2. The derivative difference between z/sqrt(1-z^2) and atanh(z) has derivative (1-z^2)^(-3/2)-(1-z^2)^(-1)>=0 and starts at zero. Integration gives dnat(z)<=1-sqrt(1-z^2). The JS packet is consequently at most 2min(a^2,b^2)/ln2, hence JS<=2d/ln2.

Thus D<=lambda+3d/ln2. The conditional drift of (3/ln2)I-H-sum previously charged lambda is D-3d/ln2-lambda<=0: it is a supermartingale, with the sign exactly as A061 states. If every positive theta>=epsilon, lambda<=log(1/epsilon)d and CI-H is a supermartingale with C=log(1/epsilon)+3/ln2. Stopping yields H(root)<=CI(root)+E[H(leaf)-CI(leaf)], retaining the terminal defect. Zero b packets are exempt and theta=1 contributes zero lambda. At fixed n every positive squared coefficient is at least 2^(-2n), giving integrability. Verdict: A061-LOG-COMPENSATION v1 accepted without alteration.

### A021 rare-core identity, remainder and the cross-ledger equality

Let g be balanced, q=2^(-k), and F=1+A_k(g-1). Its Fourier probabilities are (1-q)^2 at the empty set; q^2 at each nonempty pure-control set; and q^2 ghat(T)^2 at every nonempty data set T and every control set. The three total masses are (1-q)^2, q-q^2, q. Hence

    H(F)=qH(g)+(4q-2q^2)k-2(1-q)^2 log(1-q),
    I(F)=qI(g)+kq.

Direct edge counting gives each queried control influence q, and each data influence q times its core influence. For a disjoint t-fold balanced core, H(g)=tH(b), I(g)=tI(b). Multiplying the convergent -ln(1-q) series by 2(1-q)^2 gives 2cq-3cq^2-4c sum_(j>=3) q^j/[j(j-1)(j-2)], c=1/ln2. Bounding each tail coefficient by 1/6 yields the claimed remainder interval [-2cq^3/(3(1-q)),0], uniformly in the tensor count and seed. The ratio limits follow by dividing by q(tI(b)+k); balanced I(b)>=1 prevents degeneracy. An unbalanced seed is balanced by a fresh sign, adding one influence and no entropy. Verdict: A021-UNIFORM-RARE-CORE-EXPANSION v1 accepted.

At the active node with s controls left, put u=2^(-s). Its children are constant 1 and 1+A_(s-1)(g-1). The empty packet has Q0=1-2u+2u^2 and magnitude mismatch m0=u^2. Every other packet has one zero child coefficient, so m_T=Q_T/2; total mismatch is u. Importantly, the active child's constant coefficient 1-2u is nonnegative, including the boundary s=1. Thus signed and magnitude mismatch agree in this family. Equivalently the parent queried sector has b_T^2=m_T in every packet. It follows that A061's one-sided lambda equals A021's KL residual exactly:

    lambda_s=sum m_T log(Q_T/m_T)
             =u-u^2+u^2 log(Q0/u^2)
             =u[log(1/u)-D2(m/u || Q)].

The physical reach probability is q/u. Summing gives sum w lambda=q(k+A_k), with A_k=sum_(s<=k)2^(-s)[2s-1+log Q0]. For s=1 this summand is zero. For s>=2, 1/2<=Q0<=1, hence each bracket lies between 2s-2 and 2s-1. Therefore 0<=A_k increases to A<=3 and its tail is at most sum_(s>k)(2s-1)2^(-s)=(2k+3)2^(-k). The charged controls cost kq, terminal influence qI(g), and terminal entropy qH(g). The blind scalar rarity cost is q sum_(s<=k)s=qk(k+1)/2. The KL credit, not an extra influence allocation, removes that quadratic cost. Verdict: A021-CONTROL-FIRST-KL-LEDGER v1 accepted. The additional lambda equality is our new scoped bridge, proved without changing either source claim.

The ancillary expansions were also checked analytically: |log Q0|<=4cu for s>=2 gives the residual error bound 4cu^3. Expanding the remaining binary entropy gives the stated 5cu^3 packet-entropy bound. For the true decrement the residual difference R(u)-R(2u)/2 is nonnegative and at most 8cu^3/[3(1-2u)], giving the reported boundary-safe bound. The s=1 node uses its exact formulas rather than those expansions.

### A022 signed surgery and fixed-seed classification

For an actual flip set D, Fourier linearity gives half-difference t_S=2^(-n)sum_(v in D) f(v)chi_S(v), midpoint a_S-t_S, and selector probabilities t_S^2 and (a_S-t_S)^2. Parseval of f1_D gives sum t_S^2=|D|2^(-n)=mu. Expanding binary entropy gives L=J+R with J=sum e log(q/e)=mu[log(1/mu)-D2(e/mu||q)] and 0<=R<=mu/ln2. Zero mismatch and zero packets are handled by continuity.

For one point, e=delta^2 on every label and the normalized signed ratio r=a/(delta f(v)chi_S(v)) is an even integer whenever n>=1. Hence |r-1|>=1 and the signed mismatch equals the minority packet mass. Flipping an edge endpoint changes its influence by 2delta f(v)f(v^i). For adjacent flips, the half-difference contains 1+epsilon(-1)^(j in S); its square is 2delta^2[1+epsilon(-1)^(j in S)]. Only one Fourier half-cube is active and the shared physical edge does not change status. Original-table one-point changes require the -4delta f(v)f(v^j) interaction correction. Opposite constant children show why signed and magnitude mismatch must not generally be identified for adjacent flips.

Define Phi(r)=((r-1)^2+1)h2(1/((r-1)^2+1)). For |r|>=2, B=1-2/r+2/r^2>=1/2 gives |ln B|<=6/|r|. Also (A-1)ln(1+1/(A-1)) lies in [1-1/(2(A-1)),1], where A=(r-1)^2+1 and A-1>=r^2/4. Thus |Phi(r)-2log|r|-c|<=7/(ln2|r|)<12/|r|. All nonzero integer Walsh sums on an m>=1 cube have absolute value at least two. In a t-fold tensor, the occupied fraction is rho^t, every occupied |r|>=2^t, and the uniform-on-occupied mean log|r| is t lambda. On zero labels Phi(0)=2. Averaging proves the claimed 12(rho/2)^t error and the fixed-seed dichotomy. Sign multiplicities for a bent source follow from inversion at the flipped vertex; restricting to the active adjacent half-cube and inversion at both endpoints gives its stated multiplicities. Character padding multiplies support sparsity and integer coefficient sizes exactly as claimed. Verdict: A022-SIGNED-SURGERY-TENSOR-LOG-LEDGER v1 accepted. The asymptotic theorem is reconstructed analytically; its author's logarithm interval implementation is not independently certified by this run.

### A026 affine support and finite physical selection

For p in [0,1], h2(p)<=2sqrt(p(1-p)): the derivative after t=sqrt((1-p)/p) substitution is t-1/t-2ln(t)/ln2. Its derivative has one turning point for t>1, giving the necessary single sign change and nonnegative difference with zeros at p=0,1/2. Multiplying packetwise proves B_i<=A_i=2sum|a_T a_(T+i)|.

Our direct character-sum enumeration of all tables modulo global output sign gives exact maxima of min_(relevant i) A_i/I_i equal to 0,2,3,7/2 at n=1,2,3,4. The integer selection inequality is 4sum|W_T W_(T+i)|<=7sum W_(T+i)^2 with a positive denominator. No logarithm comparison is used.

If support lies in c+V and a relevant physical e_i is outside V, adjacent support pairs are absent, so B_i=A_i=0. The children have equal squared spectra and JS=0. If every relevant e_i lies in V, their independence implies at most dim V<=4 relevant variables. Thus finite selection applies. Physical restriction projects c+V, preserving the dimension bound. Disjoint factors give independent spectral laws, preserving factor B_i and I_i and their restriction classes. Induct with D_i<=B_i+2I_i/ln2 and exact influence conservation to get H<=(7/2+2/ln2)I. This uses physical coordinates throughout, not arbitrary Fourier linear reparametrization. Verdict: A026-AFFINE4-GOOD-COORDINATE v1 accepted.

For the second claim we independently evaluate integer Walsh sums on each level j by counting the z selected negative bits:

    W_j=sum_(w=0..32) sign(w>=24) sum_z (-1)^z binom(j,z)binom(32-j,w-z).

Then A_i/I_i=2sum_j binom(31,j)|W_jW_(j+1)| / sum_j binom(31,j)W_(j+1)^2 =2220940451/536870912>4. Its denominator independently agrees with the direct pivotal probability binom(31,23)/2^31. Permutation symmetry covers every coordinate. This refutes only K=4 amplitude-proxy selection, not true erasure selection or FEI. Verdict: A026-AMPLITUDE-K4-THRESHOLD-NOGO v1 accepted.

### A027 the proper data block and its FEI-strength boundary

For arbitrary core u on m bits let mu=Eu, d=1-mu, v=1-mu^2, p=2^(-r). The proper block W is all core variables. For every nonempty control Fourier set the fiber mass is 2p^2d and its normalized law is the squared spectrum of u-1, of entropy at most m. The empty-control fiber has R0=1-2pd+2p^2d and nonempty-core mass p^2v. Its entropy is R0 h2(p^2v/R0)+p^2vL, where L is the normalized nonempty core entropy. These are coherent actual fibers, including the shifted constant coefficient.

The nonempty fibers plus the L term are at most 2pdm since v<=2d. The remaining binary term is at most p^2[2rv+vlog(1/v)+cv]<2p: use v<=1, max v log(1/v)=1/(e ln2), rp<=1/2, p<=1/2 and (1+1/e)/ln2<2. The latter follows from ln2>=56/81 and e>8/3. Thus B_W<=p(2dm+2)<=6pm. Query cost is pI(u); terminal influence rpd comes from negative core assignments, each of probability d/2, with point-defect influence 2rp. Those leaves have H<=4I: their entropy is the constant-atom entropy plus 8p(1-p)(r-1), bounded by 4p/ln2+8p(r-1)<=8rp. The r=1 case has zero entropy. The recovered block-channel estimate J_W<=2I_W/ln2 follows from the cube two-point entropy inequality applied to absolute child amplitudes, tensorized in W; its complete proof was inspected in BLOCK_RESTRICTION_IDENTITY Section 2. This gives max(6/eta+2/ln2,4) when I(u)>=eta m. No hereditary density assumption is smuggled into this one-step theorem. Verdict: A027-DATA-FIRST-PROPER-BLOCK v1 accepted.

Put delta=d/2. The squared spectrum of u-1 has empty mass delta and nonempty mass 1-delta. Summing the same fibers exactly gives

    B_W/p=vL+4delta(1-p)h2(delta)+(R0/p)h2(p^2v/R0).

The last term tends to zero as p->0 for a fixed core. For mu>=0, edge isoperimetry gives I(u)>=2delta log(1/delta). When delta<=1/4, h2(v)<=vlog(e/v)<=4delta log(e/(4delta))<=2I(u); when delta in [1/4,1/2], the concavity chord gives I(u)>=1 and h2(v)<=1. The source's isoperimetric induction is valid: within-half boundaries plus at least the difference of half-set sizes reduce exactly to h2(a/(a+b))>=2a/(a+b). Consequently a universal block bound with K implies H(u)<=KI(u)+h2(v)<= (K+2)I(u); complementing extends it to negative means. Conversely Parseval gives v<=I(u), the exceptional row contributes <=3I(u), and 4delta h2(delta)<=2I(u). Hence B_W/p<=H(u)+5I(u), proving the C+5 implication. Verdict: A027-CORE-BLOCK-HARDNESS v1 accepted. Properness of this block does not make its unrestricted payment easier than FEI.

### A062 dimension-limit obstruction

The quadratic two-bit sign has four probabilities 1/4, entropy 2 and influence 1. Balance the product of 2^(k-1) such independent factors with a fresh sign. Then H(g_k)=2^k and I(g_k)=2^(k-1)+1. Activate with k fresh controls. A021's directly reconstructed spectrum gives root influence 1/2+(k+1)2^(-k)<=3/2. Under control-first stopping, terminal influence is (2^(k-1)+1)1_(A_k) and entropy is 2^k 1_(A_k). On coupled infinite fair controls they tend to zero almost surely because the first negative control occurs at finite time almost surely, but expectations are 1/2+2^(-k) and 1. Tail masses therefore stay positive for arbitrary cutoffs: uniform integrability fails. This is a family across increasing dimensions, not an optional-stopping failure on any one fixed cube. Verdict: A062-BOOLEAN-DIMENSION-LIMIT-OBSTRUCTION v1 accepted.

### Exact first unsupported universal line

Neither the stopped identities nor these acceptances establish a policy on every Boolean function with dimension-free paid erasure/logarithmic cost and paid terminal entropy. A021's terminal qH(g) preserves arbitrary core difficulty; A027 proves its unrestricted core-block payment equivalent to FEI; A062 forbids disposing of that difficulty through almost-sure terminal limits alone. A026's hereditary affine-dimension hypothesis and A061's fixed density hypothesis cannot be deleted. The missing universal line remains: construct a realizable stopping tree or debt satisfying D_v<=C d_v+Q_v-E[Q_child|history], with root and terminal debt both paid from the same physical influence reserve.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. NPN invariance: a physical signed permutation

Let pi be a permutation of n input coordinates, eta an input sign vector and epsilon an output sign. Define

    f'(x)=epsilon f(eta_1 x_(pi(1)),...,eta_n x_(pi(n))).

Changing variables in the uniform Fourier expectation proves, for every S,

    f'hat(pi(S))=epsilon (product_(j in S) eta_j) fhat(S).

Thus the squared spectrum is only relabelled by pi. Physical input and output negations change coefficient signs, not squared masses. For every coordinate j, the two spectral packet masses without and with j map respectively to the packet masses without and with pi(j). Hence B_(pi(j))(f')=B_j(f) and Inf_(pi(j))(f')=Inf_j(f). Relevant coordinates are also relabelled. Consequently

    min_(i:Inf_i>0) B_i/Inf_i,
    max_(i:Inf_i>0) B_i/Inf_i, H/I,

are NPN invariant whenever their ratios are defined. Constant functions are handled separately, not by dividing zero by zero. Embedded dummy variables do not create relevant candidates.

This also preserves physical restrictions: fixing x_(pi(j))=s corresponds to fixing old input j=eta_j s, followed by the residual signed permutation and output sign. Thus admissible children and fair branch weights are preserved, not merely the abstract parent spectrum.

The NPN theorem justifies one representative per genuinely complete NPN orbit in A090's proposed census. It does not certify catalogue coverage, the stated count 616,126, duplicate removal, or extremizer logarithm comparisons. Those are separate exact-certificate obligations. Arbitrary invertible F2 coordinate changes and multiplication by a character are not NPN operations. Character multiplication XOR-translates Fourier labels and can replace Inf_i by 1-Inf_i; it does not generally preserve these ratios. Therefore neither larger affine equivalence nor a Fourier-support quotient is admitted by this proof.

### 2. Exact coherent decrement from the physical children

Let F=g(h_1,...,h_m) on disjoint physical blocks with every h_l balanced. Fix coordinate i in block j and write g=a+y_j b. Pointwise Booleanity gives ab=0 and a²+b²=1. In the uniform normalized Fourier basis,

    ||a||²=1-q, ||b||²=q=Inf_j(g), <a,b>=0.

Write alpha=h_jhat({i}), x=alpha². The actual inner children h_+ and h_- have means +alpha and -alpha because h_j is balanced. Their nonconstant Fourier vectors nu_+ and nu_- each have squared mass 1-x and raw entropy H(h_±)-x ln(1/x).

For a remaining outer set T, put C_T=sum_(l in T) H(h_l). Balancedness means each selected inner block occupies a nonempty physical frequency sector, so different outer sets occupy disjoint sectors. Raw product entropy satisfies E(v tensor w)=||w||² E(v)+||v||² E(w). The root entropy is therefore

    H(F)=E(a)+E(b)+qH(h_j)+sum_T(a_T²+b_T²)C_T.

At physical child sign sigma, the empty block-j sector has the signed coefficient vector a+sigma alpha b; the nonempty block-j sectors have b tensor nu_sigma. For each individual T the total weight of C_T is

    (a_T+sigma alpha b_T)²+(1-x)b_T²
       =a_T²+b_T²+2sigma alpha a_T b_T.

The last term must remain in each child. Its cancellation occurs only after the fair physical average, even though the total inner product <a,b> already vanishes. Thus

    H(F_sigma)=E(a+sigma alpha b)+(1-x)E(b)
               +q[H(h_sigma)-xln(1/x)]
               +sum_T[a_T²+b_T²+2sigma alpha a_Tb_T]C_T.

Subtracting the average of these two actual children gives

    D_i(F)=qD_i(h_j)+R(alpha),
    R=qxln(1/x)+E(a)+xE(b)-[E(a+alpha b)+E(a-alpha b)]/2.

This directly reconstructs the previously unaudited D-part. No child is replaced by an incoherent square-mixture, and neither noisy outer function nor its normalized coefficient vector is treated as a Boolean leaf.

For completeness, in a nonempty residual block-j frequency U, the physical queried packet is b_T times the corresponding inner packet, with the other-block product masses. Its conditional entropy sums to qB_i(h_j). In the inner empty-residual packet, h_j has masses 0,x and zero erasure, while F has masses a_T²,xb_T². Therefore

    B_i(F)=qB_i(h_j)+L_j(x),
    L_j(x)=sum_T s_T h_nat(xb_T²/s_T), s_T=a_T²+xb_T².

The algebraic identity L=E(a)+xE(b)+qxln(1/x)-E(s) yields R=L+J, where

    J=E(s)-[E(a+alpha b)+E(a-alpha b)]/2.

It also gives the useful exact channel split JS_i(F)=qJS_i(h_j)+J.

### 3. Channel bound, equal mass, and all endpoints

Both coherent vectors a±alpha b have squared mass M=1-q+qx, exactly the same as sum s_T, because <a,b>=0. If M>0, normalize them by sqrt(M). Their squared spectra P_+ and P_- are probability laws with average s/M. The raw entropy normalization term Mln(1/M) cancels, giving J=M JS_nat(P_+,P_-)>=0.

For one packet with z=2|uv|/(u²+v²), let d(z)=[(1+z)ln(1+z)+(1-z)ln(1-z)]/2. The difference of derivatives z/sqrt(1-z²)-atanh z is nonnegative because its derivative is (1-z²)^(-3/2)-(1-z²)^(-1)>=0 and it starts at zero. Integration yields d(z)<=1-sqrt(1-z²), including |z|=1 by continuity. Substituting u=a_T and v=alpha b_T gives

    0<=J<=2sum_T min(a_T²,xb_T²)<=2qx.

The common mass is indispensable. Without it, a raw subprobability entropy difference could carry uncancelled normalization terms. Here the normalized channel is valid even though the noisy outer generally is not Boolean.

Concavity of -rln r and s=(1-x)a²+x(a²+b²) give

    E(s)>=(1-x)E(a²)+xE(a²+b²),
    0<=L<=xB_j(g)+qxln(1/x).

Hence 0<=R<=xB_j(g)+qxln(1/x)+2qx. The notation E(a²) in this display means entropy of the mass vector, equivalently E(a) in the coefficient notation above.

For q=0, b=0 and all extra charges vanish. For x=0, L=J=R=0. For x=1, Booleanity forces h_j=±x_i; the inner decrement is zero and R=D_j(g). If M=0, necessarily q=1,x=0,a=0, and every relevant noisy vector vanishes, so L=J=R=0 without dividing by M. These cover the exact zero and bias endpoints in the v1 statement. Negative alpha only swaps the coherent child vectors; fair averaged identities and bounds are unchanged.

Verdict: A057-COHERENT-ERASURE-SPLIT v1 HOLDS, including its D and J clauses. Its root aggregate follows by summing the exact B identities and L bounds, with W_1(h_j)=sum_i alpha_(j,i)² and influence factorization I(F)=sum_j q_j I(h_j). The proof uses no full-FEI hypothesis.

### 4. Nonreusing alpha logarithm on the same physical tree

The Boolean physical half-difference delta_i h_j lies in {-1,0,1}, has mean alpha and expected absolute value Inf_i(h_j). Thus |alpha|<=Inf_i(h_j). Since the other inner outputs are independent fair signs and disjoint from the queried block, physical pivotality factorizes:

    Inf_i(F)=qInf_i(h_j).

The scalar function rln(1/r²), r in [0,1], has maximum 2/e at r=e^-1. Consequently

    qalpha²ln(1/alpha²)<=(2/e)q|alpha|
                           <=(2/e)Inf_i(F),
    qalpha²<=Inf_i(F).

For a finite adaptive physical tree, each next fresh coordinate is chosen from observed history and independent policy randomness. Unobserved signs remain fair. Averaging surviving edge disagreement indicators gives I(f_v)=Inf_(i_v)(f_v)+E[I(child)|history]. Multiplying by physical reach weights and summing cancels each intermediate surviving reserve exactly once:

    I(root)=sum_internal w_v Inf_(i_v)(f_v)+E I(leaf).

Nonnegative charges over any subset of independently certified represented nodes are therefore at most the full queried budget. Summing the scalar bound gives

    sum w_v q_v alpha_v²ln(1/alpha_v²)<=(2/e)sum w_v Inf_(i_v)(f_v)
                                                <=(2/e)I(root).

Combining with the node R bound gives sum w_v R_v<=sum w_v alpha_v² B_(j_v)(g_v)+(2+2/e)sum w_v Inf_(i_v)(f_v). In bits the logarithmic constant is 2/(e ln2), and the second influence coefficient is (2+2/e)/ln2. There is no additional formula-depth allocation and no repeated payment from the original root influence.

The theorem assumes a certified balanced disjoint representation at each included actual node. It does not assert that the root representation survives restriction. An inner majority has nonzero singleton alpha, so its two physical children have means ±alpha and cease to be balanced. Those nodes need recentering or a new representation; the original balanced proof cannot simply be iterated. The checker deliberately omits biased nodes from the represented bank and retains their queried costs and terminal entropy in the physical telescope.

Verdict: A057-NONREUSING-ALPHA-LOG-PAYMENT v1 HOLDS at exactly its stated subset-of-represented-nodes scope. This completes the requested D/J/log audit without a repair contribution to either A057 claim.

### 5. Narrowed A021 acceptance and corrected v2

CORRECTION_CONSUMED.md records the exact affected earlier source and verdict. My stage-1 acceptance was wrong for the v1 series sign and its derivative decrement-remainder sign. The actual coefficient of q^j for j>=3 in (1-2q+q²)sum_(l>=1)q^l/l is 1/j-2/(j-1)+1/(j-2)=2/[j(j-1)(j-2)]>0. Thus

    -2(1-q)²log2(1-q)=2cq-3cq²+R(q),
    R(q)=4c sum_(j>=3)q^j/[j(j-1)(j-2)],
    0<R(q)<=2cq³/[3(1-q)], c=1/ln2.

The endpoint q=1/2 already disproves R<=0: R=(2ln2-1)/(4ln2)>0 because ln2>1/2. The corrected entropy ratio interval follows by dividing the positive-tail bounds by q(tI(b)+k). That denominator is positive for the stated balanced cores. The tail is independent of tensor size, so the original ratio limits hold.

For u=2^-s, s>=2, G_s=R(u)-R(2u)/2 has negative coefficients 4c(1-2^(j-1))u^j/[j(j-1)(j-2)], hence -8cu³/[3(1-2u)]<=G_s<0. This confirms the v2 decrement sign. The original exact spectrum/influence formulas, KL ledger, and A087 lambda/KL identity require no remainder-sign assumption and remain accepted. KL here denotes base-two Kullback-Leibler divergence, not a Rényi order; the equality is always between the displayed formulas, not similarly named unrelated debts.

The correction is authored by A021 with the B05 lead credited for the sign repair. A087 supplies an independent reconstruction and rational certificates, not a new repair to v2. Verdict: corrected A021-UNIFORM-RARE-CORE-EXPANSION v2 HOLDS. The separate new odd-face claim is not accepted by this run.

### 6. First unsupported universal line

Even after the logarithm is paid, the exact represented-tree identity is

    H(root)=E H(leaf)+sum w_v q_v D_(i_v)(h_(v,j_v))+sum w_v R_v.

The remaining sum of terminal entropy, inner decrements, and scaled outer erasure sum w_v alpha_v² B_(j_v)(g_v) has no proved absolute influence price. Nor is a valid balanced or recentered representation supplied for all biased descendants. At alpha=1, R is the arbitrary outer decrement, so declaring the full remainder locally paid would recover the already false all-coordinate restriction estimate. The first missing universal statement is a signed, nonreusing recentered descendant reserve that pays those terms from actual queried plus terminal influence.

# A089: exact existential repair of the every-coordinate obstruction

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All entropies are in bits. For a Boolean sign f, let p_S=fhat(S)^2,
I_i=sum_{S contains i}p_S, and B_i=H(S_i|S_without_i). Define
Phi(u,v)=(u+v)h2(v/(u+v)), with its continuous boundary values.
We compute min_{i:I_i>0} B_i/I_i on the two families specified by the
B18 lead. The proofs cover all their parameters, not an extrapolation
from the finite checks.

**Result, author proved; independent review pending:** On B05 sparse bent
point surgery the minimizing type is a data coordinate for k=4 and a
control coordinate for even k>=6. An exact sharp uniform constant is
K_B05=psi(57/262144), where

    psi(t)=2-2t+Phi((1-t)^2,t^2)/t.

In particular K_B05 < 2+7/1024. This also covers any number r>=2k of
controls. On the A062 activation family, the minimizing coordinate is
the balancing sign, and the exact sharp uniform constant is

    K_A062=Phi(1,1/4)=(5/4)log2(5)-2 < 1.

Its best-coordinate ratio tends to zero. Neither family obstructs the
existential good-coordinate form of C018. Full FEI remains OPEN.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

Read the complete EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.json, revision
2026-10-11-execution-v2; report README and CURRENT_STATUS; claim,
obstruction, packet and next-action indices; campaign plan and recovered
run navigation. Read BOARD.md and B18 STREAM_NOTES.md v1, 22:45 UTC,
whose A089 target specifies these two families. Read supplement
CAMPAIGN.md, CLAIMS.md (C005/C013/C018/C019), COUNTEREXAMPLES.md,
SEED_LEMMAS.md, NATIVE_MAPPING.md and source inventory. Mathematical
source actually consumed: sources/ERASURE_REDUCTION.md sections 1-4,
including its exact B_i formula and physical influence identity.

Original failed statement: B05 REPORT.md,
20261011T193057Z-fleet01-B05-rare-restrictions, sections 2-6;
registry pins its original commit 80cc2e5027efa56d79c4ecdf7d4949759ef2068b
and the independent B19 acceptance at
7955d427222392911dc6af2e4ee3aa8aab67ac8c. Read A021 rare-core report
and A062 REPORT.md, 20261011T220423Z-fleet05-A062-influence-compensator,
including the exact construction in its dimension-limit witness.

The supplied four paper text versions were inspected for context and

percolation 30pp, section 2, Lemma 2.1 (terminal refinement cost).

is imported. Local file hashes are in out/SOURCE_INVENTORY.json and

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Preserve the failed statement and witness

The original false universal statement is: fixed finite K,A,B>=0 exist
such that, at **every** actual Boolean coordinate split,

    L <= K d + A V_post + B M_inf.

Here L=B_i, d=I_i, V_post=sum_W q_W(theta_W-d)^2, and
M_inf=sum_j (I_j(child+)-I_j(child-))^2. B05's actual family has, at its
selector y, d=2^(-3k), B_y/d=k+log2(e)+o(1),
V_post/d<=4*2^(-k), and M_inf/d=12k*2^(-3k). It refutes every fixed
choice of these prices. The first invalid line in any proof using that
helper is its assertion at every coordinate. Replacing "every" by
"some" is a different quantifier and needs a new proof.

A062's separate failed inference is that a uniformly bounded root
influence permits dropping the terminal reserve in a dimension limit.
Its activation witness has root I<=3/2 but expected terminal influence
tending to 1/2 and expected terminal entropy equal to 1, although the
coupled terminal random variables tend almost surely to zero. The
coordinate result here does not repair that limit exchange.

### 2. Elementary entropy tools

For u,v>=0, Phi(u,v)<=u+v and Phi(u,v)>=2min(u,v), by the upper bound
and chord lower bound for h2. If u>=v>0, then

    Phi(u,v) >= v[log2(u/v)+1].                         (E1)

Indeed u log2(1+v/u)>=v, since log2(1+x)>=x for 0<=x<=1.
For any u,v with u+v<=1,

    Phi(u,v) <= v log2(e/v).                           (E2)

This follows from h2(theta)<=theta log2(e/theta).
Writing c=log2(e)=1/ln2, we use 7/5<c<3/2. For completeness,
the positive series ln2=2 sum_{j>=0}(1/3)^(2j+1)/(2j+1)
gives ln2>2/3 and ln2<=2/3+1/36=25/36<5/7.

The elementary inequalities

    1-h2((1+z)/2) >= c z^2/2,
    1-h2((1+z)/2) <= c(1-sqrt(1-z^2))

follow respectively by integrating atanh(z)>=z and
atanh(z)<=z/sqrt(1-z^2); the latter proof is reproduced in the
erasure source. These inequalities provide every all-parameter sign
used below; floating logs are unnecessary.

### 3. B05: Fourier expansion and all coordinate ratios

Use 0/1 physical bits with characters (-1)^(S dot x). Let k>=4 be even,
delta=2^(-k), s=sqrt(delta)=2^(-k/2), r>=2k, p=2^(-r),
m=s-delta, a=1-m, and t=pa. Let b be the quadratic bent sign on k
bits, D the indicator of x=0, A the indicator of z=0 on r controls,
and C the indicator y=1. The actual sign is

    f(x,z,y)=1+A(z)(h(x,y)-1),
    h(x,y)=b(x)-2D(x)C(y).

At y=0 this is G; at y=1 it is H from the original B05 construction.
The Fourier coefficients c_T of b are +-s, with c_empty=c_{singleton}=s.
The core coefficients are hhat(T,empty)=c_T-delta and
hhat(T,{y})=delta. Therefore fhat(empty)=1-pa; on every other index
with core index empty and nonempty control index the coefficient is
-pa; on every nonempty core index it is p times its core coefficient.

Let

    b_x = 1/2-delta/2 + Phi((s-delta)^2,(s+delta)^2)/(4delta).

All data coordinates have identical ratios, and direct spectral pairing
gives

    I_x = p(1/2+delta),
    B_x = p(b_x-2m^2)+(p-p^2)Phi(a^2,m^2)
          +Phi((1-pa)^2,p^2m^2).                      (B05-X)

To see the multiplicities in b_x, translation of the bent spectrum by
one data coordinate has 2^(k-2) same-sign pairs and 2^(k-2)
opposite-sign pairs. The sum of the coefficient over the same-sign
pairs is 1/2, since sum_T c_T=b(0)=1 and opposite pairs cancel.
Thus their mass after subtracting delta is 1/2-3delta/2;
the y-containing pairs add delta, and the opposite pairs contribute
Phi((s-delta)^2,(s+delta)^2)/(4delta). This is b_x.
Activation replaces the empty-core coefficient in **all** control
fibers, giving the two correction terms in (B05-X). The first finite
check caught my initial omission of the nonempty-control fibers;
out/FIRST_CHECK_FAILURE.md preserves that implementation correction.
Data edge counting gives I_x: b has I_x=1/2, and flipping its origin
increases each data influence by 2delta in the y=1 child only.

Every control coordinate has

    I_z=t,
    B_z=2t-2t^2+Phi((1-t)^2,t^2),
    B_z/I_z=psi(t).                                   (B05-Z)

For nonempty core indices both control-edge coefficients are equal;
their total B contribution is p(1-m^2). The nonempty residual control
indices contribute (p-2p^2)a^2, and the empty residual pair contributes
Phi((1-t)^2,t^2). The sum is (B05-Z).

For the selector put e=p^2delta^2 and
N_+=(2^k+2^(k/2))/2-1, N_-=(2^k-2^(k/2))/2. Its exact closed form is

    I_y=p delta,
    B_y=2^r[N_+ Phi(p^2(s-delta)^2,e)
              +N_- Phi(p^2(s+delta)^2,e)]
        +(2^r-1)Phi(p^2a^2,e)+Phi((1-pa)^2,e).         (B05-Y)

These formulas are finite closed expressions in k,r and logarithms.
They do not enumerate an exponentially large cube to state the answer.

### 4. B05: determine the minimizing coordinate and sharp constant

The selector's nonempty-data fibers have midpoint p(c_T-delta).
Their q/e >=1/(2delta), because (1-s)^2+s^2>=1/2. Their total
mismatch mass is (1-delta)I_y. Thus

    B_y/I_y >= (1-delta)(k-1) >=45/16.                (B05-Y-lower)

By Phi>=2min, psi(t)>=2. Also t<=p<=delta^2 and a>=13/16>1/2.
Using (E2) and -2t<=0,

    psi(t) <= 2+t[c+2log2(1/t)]
           <= 2+p(4+2r)
           <= 2+delta^2(4+4k).                       (B05-Z-upper)

The last step uses that 2^(-r)(4+2r) decreases in integer r.

The entropy deficit inequalities in section 2 applied with
z=2s/(1+delta) yield

    1-c delta <= b_x <= 1-c delta/(1+delta).          (B05-core)

For k=4, m=3/16 and a=13/16. In Phi(a^2,m^2), the ratio
(a^2+m^2)/m^2=178/9<20 has logarithm <9/2, since 20^2<2^9.
Hence Phi(a^2,m^2)<=6m^2. Moreover p^2m^2<=delta^5,
and monotonicity of v log2(e/v) for v<=1 gives

    Phi((1-pa)^2,p^2m^2)/p <= delta^3(c+5k).

Here (E2) bounds the left side by
p m^2 log2(e/(p^2m^2)), which increases in both p and m^2 on
0<p<=delta^2, 0<m^2<=delta. Its partial derivatives are positive
because p^2m^2<=delta^5<=2^(-20). Thus substitution of their upper
bounds is valid even when r>2k; dividing an upper bound for Phi
by a smaller p alone would not justify this step.

With (B05-core),

    B_x/p <= 1-(7/5)/17+4(9/256)+22/4096 < 9/8.

Since I_x/p=9/16, its ratio is strictly below 2. Both other types
are >=2, so every data coordinate is a minimizer at k=4.

For even k>=6, a/m>1/s, so log2(a^2/m^2)>k, and (E1) gives
Phi(a^2,m^2)>=(k+1)m^2. Dropping the nonnegative last term in
(B05-X), and using c<3/2, shows

    B_x/I_x >= 2 + delta/(1/2+delta)
       * [((k+1)(1-p)-2)(1-s)^2-7/2].

The bracket is >=(7(1-1/4096)-2)(49/64)-7/2>8/25;
the positive factors increase with k, while p decreases. Since
1/2+delta<=33/64<8/15, the data ratio is >2+(3/5)delta.
But (4+4k)delta<=28/64=7/16, so the control ratio is
<=2+(7/16)delta. The selector lower bound also exceeds this.
Therefore **controls are exactly the minimizing type for k>=6**.

For sharpness, psi is strictly increasing on 0<t<=1/256. Indeed,
with Q=(1-t)^2+t^2, direct differentiation gives

    psi'(t) = -2 + log2(Q/t^2)
                    -(1-t^2)/t^2 * log2(Q/(1-t)^2)
             >= -2+2log2((1-t)/t)-c(1+t)/(1-t) >0.

Here log2((1-t)/t)>=log2(255)>7 and
c(1+t)/(1-t)<(3/2)(257/255)<2. As k increases through even values,
t(k,2k)=delta^2(1-s+delta) strictly decreases: the delta^2 factor
shrinks by 1/16, while a grows by a factor <16/13.
At fixed k, t decreases with r. Thus the maximum control minimum
is attained at k=6,r=12, where a=57/64 and t=57/262144.
At k=4 the minimum is <2 whereas psi(t)>2 for small positive t
(also follows from strict increase and lim_{t->0}psi(t)=2).
Consequently the claimed K_B05 is the exact sharp constant over
all even k>=4,r>=2k, and K_B05<2+28/4096=2+7/1024.

The limiting minimum is 2. This confirms that B05's logarithm is
localized to its selector; it is not an existential-selection no-go.

### 5. A062: exact minimum, sharp constant, and vanishing limit

Let k>=1, M=2^(k-1), q=2^(-k), and

    g_k(y_0,x)=(-1)^(y_0) product_{j=1}^M (-1)^(x_{2j-1}x_{2j}),
    f_k=1+A_k(z)(g_k-1).

The core is balanced, H(g_k)=2M=2^k and I(g_k)=M+1. All its
nonzero coefficients contain y_0; their magnitudes are 2^(-M).
Let v=4^(-M). The squared coefficient at {y_0} is v.

Every control has I_z=q and ratio psi(q)>=2. Every bent data
coordinate has I_x=q/2 and B_x=q, ratio exactly 2: in each
y_0-containing control fiber the paired squared coefficients are equal,
and y_0-absent pairs contribute zero. The balancing sign has

    I_{y_0}=q,
    B_{y_0}=(q-q^2)Phi(1,v)+Phi((1-q)^2,q^2v),
    R_k=B_{y_0}/I_{y_0}
       =(1-q)Phi(1,v)+Phi((1-q)^2,q^2v)/q.             (A062-sign)

Only the zero bent-data index pairs with the y_0-absent activation
coefficients. There are 2^k-1 nonempty control fibers and one empty
fiber; all other sign pairs have a zero member. This proves the formula.

For k=1, q=1/2,v=1/4. Homogeneity of Phi gives
R_1=Phi(1,1/4)=(5/4)log2(5)-2. The exact integer comparisons
2^11<5^5<2^12 imply 3/4<R_1<1.

For k>=2, v<=1/16 and q<=1/4. Since log2(1+v)<1,

    Phi(1,v)<=v(3+2M),
    Phi((1-q)^2,q^2v)/q <=qv(2+2k+2M).

Thus R_k<=v(4+k+3M)<=4(M+1)4^(-M)<=3/4, using M>=k.
The function (M+1)4^(-M) decreases at integer M>=2. Therefore
R_k<1 for every k, it is strictly smaller than both other coordinate
types, and sup_k R_k=R_1. Also R_k<=4(M+1)4^(-M)->0.
This is an exact closed-form best-coordinate computation and an
optimal uniform bound for this particular all-dimensional family.

### 6. Useful application and exact remaining gap

This repair restores the **root selection** application that the
every-coordinate helper could not justify. For B05 choose a data bit
at k=4 and a control bit at k>=6; for A062 choose the balancing sign.
Using C005, the root entropy decrement is bounded by
(K_B05+2/ln2)I_i or (K_A062+2/ln2)I_i, respectively.
This invokes no variance credit and does not reuse influence.

It does not prove an induction over these root families: arbitrary
restrictions can leave the quantified class. In particular an A062
sign restriction produces two unbalanced activated bent cores, so a
policy must still pay their entropy. The exact first unsupported
universal line is that every nonconstant restriction reached by the
chosen policy has a uniformly paid good coordinate or a certified
terminal reserve. A small root erasure ratio cannot remove arbitrary
leaf entropy. No universal B<=KI conclusion is asserted.

# A089 stage 2: lexicographic limits and the raw composition repair

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Following B18 STREAM_NOTES v2, compute the relevant-coordinate minimum
E(f)=min_i B_i(f)/Inf_i(f) for lexicographic signs near minority measure
1/10, and decide whether raw recursive compositions of the five-bit
t=3 sign can produce growing E(f). All inputs are independent uniform
physical bits, output signs are Boolean, and entropy is in bits.
Write Phi(a,b)=(a+b)h2(b/(a+b)), with continuous zero conventions,
d_i=Inf_i, and B_i=H(S_i|S_without_i) for the squared Fourier law.

Three results are proved locally, pending independent review:

1. For f_n=-1 exactly on numerical indices <floor(2^n/10), every n>=5
has its E(f_n) attained exactly at the three most significant coordinates:

       E(f_n)=psi(2 floor(2^n/10)/2^n),
       psi(d)=2-2d+Phi((1-d)^2,d^2)/d.

   The exact limit is (17 log2 17-56)/5, enclosed numerically as
   2.697373660251154... by the rigorous saved interval.
2. The unqualified limit assertion using only t/2^n ->1/10 is false.
For m>=5, L=m^2, n=m+L and t=2^L floor(2^m/10)+1, the minimum is
attained at the newly relevant terminal coordinates and tends to 2.
Both sequences are actual lexicographic Boolean signs with the same
limiting minority measure.
3. Let F_0=x and F_d=f_5(F_{d-1}^{(0)},...,F_{d-1}^{(4)}), with five
disjoint copies and f_5=-1 on indices 0,1,2. Encode a +1 child as
physical bit 0 and a -1 child as bit 1 at each outer gate. Then
E(F_d)<3 for every d>=1, in physical dimension 5^d. An explicit leaf
path T,B,T,B,... certifies the bound, where T is one of the three high
gate coordinates and B one of the two low coordinates. The complete
finite profiles at depths 1..4 give minima respectively about
2.695887520153101, 2.187135307982910, 2.195676865035359,
2.190922624841381. These decimals are displays; interval endpoints are
the certificates. Raw recursion cannot cross 3.39 or give unbounded E.

Balanced lifts or complemented variants at selected levels are different
families; no conclusion about all such variants is asserted. Full FEI is OPEN.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

Read BOARD.md snapshot 2026-10-12 04:39 UTC, including the omitted
middle portion in a separate read, B18 STREAM_NOTES.md v2 of
2026-10-11 23:34 UTC, own STATUS.md and LEARNED.md, and the complete
EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.json revision 2026-10-11-execution-v2. Previous
campaign/source/paper reads remain pinned in the first-stage source
inventory; no new whole-manuscript audit is claimed this stage.

Read B18 lead code/lex_check.py for its exact numerical-index convention

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Failed statements and original counterexamples remain visible

The earlier failed every-coordinate helper was

    B_i <= K d_i + A V_post,i + B M_inf,i at every Boolean split.

On the original B05 selector, d_i=2^(-3k), B_i/d_i=k+log2(e)+o(1),
V_post,i/d_i<=4*2^(-k), and M_inf,i/d_i=12k*2^(-3k). Thus all fixed
finite prices fail there. Stage 1 repaired only the existential family
selection, and A001 has independently accepted its r=2k B05 subclaim.
This stage follows the lead's new target rather than repeating that
refutation or converting its root result into a hereditary theorem.

The new candidate statement being corrected is:

    t_n/2^n ->1/10 forces E(lex(n,t_n)) ->2.6974... .

It is correct for canonical dyadic truncations, with the exact constant
below, but false with only that hypothesis. Section 5 gives the actual
Boolean countersequence and its different limit. This was a question
from the lead, not a claim that the lead had already proved.

For composition, the unsupported shortcut would be substituting
f_5 recursively into a balanced-composition identity. The seed has
mean 13/16; at level two its inner gates are biased, so the first
balanced assumption already fails. Section 6 retains that bias exactly.

### 2. Scalar estimates and an exact coefficient ledger

Let c=log2(e). The elementary estimates used are

    Phi(a,b)<=b log2(e(a+b)/b),
    Phi(a,b)>=b[log2(a/b)+c],                        (1)

for positive a,b. The upper estimate is the binary-entropy upper
bound. For the lower estimate write Phi=b log2(a/b)+(a+b)log2(1+b/a)
and use ln(1+x)>=x/(1+x). Also 7/5<c<3/2: the positive atanh series
at 1/3 gives 2/3<ln2<=25/36<5/7. Joint concavity and homogeneity
of Phi give sum_r Phi(a_r,b_r)<=Phi(sum a_r,sum b_r) by log-sum.

For an inner Boolean g with mean mu and singleton coefficient c_i,
define the nonconstant spectral-edge reserve

    R_i(g)=B_i(g)-Phi(mu^2,c_i^2)>=0.

For a physical extension with one constant child c0 in {+1,-1} and
one child g, Fourier pairing gives

    mu'=(mu+c0)/2, c_i'=c_i/2, d_i'=d_i/2,
    R_i(g')=R_i(g)/2+Phi((mu-c0)^2,c_i^2)/4.          (2)

Every nonempty remainder pair duplicates with one-quarter mass.
The empty remainder pair creates the constant packet
Phi((mu+c0)^2,c_i^2)/4 and the nonempty new-coordinate packet
Phi((mu-c0)^2,c_i^2)/4. Removing the first proves (2).
No independence of spectral coordinates is assumed.

For an r-control activation 1+A_r(h-1), p=2^(-r), the same pairing
gives the grouped version

    R_i'=p R_i(h)+(p-p^2)Phi((1-mu_h)^2,c_i(h)^2).  (3)

The empty-core coefficient changes in every control fiber. This is
the correction that mattered in stage 1. Input reflections and output
negation leave the squared Fourier law and each physical influence
unchanged, so (3) applies when h=-g with reflected input variables.

All lex signs and all raw recursive signs here are unate. A derivative
in a fixed coordinate has one constant sign, hence |c_i|=d_i. Put
J_i=R_i/d_i. Equation (2) then becomes the exact nonnegative ledger

    J_i'=J_i+Phi((mu-c0)^2,d_i^2)/(2d_i).            (4)

Unateness here is used to identify the singleton coefficient with
influence, not to assume an FEI bound.

### 3. Closed form for every finite lexicographic coordinate

Let f=1-2*1{sum_i 2^i x_i<t} on n physical 0/1 bits.
For coordinate i, initialize

    alpha_i=(t mod 2^(i+1))/2^(i+1),
    u=1-2alpha_i, d=1-|u|, J=2-2d.

If d=0 the coordinate is irrelevant. Otherwise, for each j=i+1,...,n-1,
with c_j=1-2*(the j-th bit of t), update

    J <- J+Phi((u-c_j)^2,d^2)/(2d),
    u <- (u+c_j)/2, d <- d/2.                       (5)

At the end,

    Inf_i=d,
    B_i/Inf_i=J+Phi(u^2,d^2)/d.                    (6)

This finite sum is a closed expression in t,n and logarithms, requiring
no 2^n truth table. At its initial split, one child is constant and the
other is a residual threshold; the queried influence is d and its
erasure is 2d-2d^2+Phi((1-d)^2,d^2). Thus its nonconstant reserve
is exactly J=2-2d. Each higher bit gives (4), proving (5)-(6).

Integer Walsh transforms and direct physical edges independently
verify this formula on every one of the 120 threshold truth tables in
dimensions 1..6, including irrelevant coordinates and both threshold
orientations. The all-dimensional formula is established by the derivation.

### 4. Canonical truncations: determine the minimum for all n>=5

Define g_m^- as the lex sign with negative support size floor(2^m/5)
and g_m^+ with negative support size ceil(2^m/5). For m>=2, every
nonconstant such gate has mean mu in [1/2,3/4], except the constant
g_2^-, which has no relevant coordinates. The bounds follow from
1/8<=floor/ceil(2^m/5)/2^m<=1/4 in the nonconstant cases; the few
m=2,3,4,5 cases are exact and the general inequalities follow once
2^m/5-1>=2^m/8.

For m>=4, both gates have a leading zero run of length two and

    g_m^sigma = 1+A_2(-g_(m-2)^(-sigma)(reflected inputs)-1).

This follows because complementing the inner interval of size
floor/ceil(4*2^(m-2)/5), then reflecting its inputs, gives the opposite
rounding of 2^(m-2)/5. At a leading control, d=1-mu and J=2mu.

Claim: each relevant coordinate of every nonconstant g_m^sigma,
m>=2, either has d=1-mu and J=2mu, or has J>27/10.
The base m=2,3 gates are point defects on two or three relevant
variables, with possible dummies, and have only the first type.
For induction, a coordinate of the first type in the child has
d=1-mu_child in [1/4,1/2]. By (3), its new nonconstant ratio is

    W(d)=2-2d+(3/4)Phi((2-d)^2,d^2)/d.

Using (1) and c>7/5,

    W(d)>L(d)=2-(19/20)d+(3/2)d log2((2-d)/d).

The function d log((2-d)/d) has negative second derivative, so L is
concave. It is therefore bounded below by the smaller endpoint value:

    L(1/4)=141/80+(3/8)log2(7)>27/10,
    L(1/2)=61/40+(3/4)log2(3)>27/10.

The exact comparisons 7^2>2^5 and 3^12>2^19 prove those signs.
Coordinates whose J already exceeds 27/10 remain so because (3)
adds a nonnegative amount. New leading controls have the first type.
If the child is constant only the new controls are relevant. This
proves the claim. The balanced one-bit child arises only in the
separately checked m=3 point-defect base.

Now a_n=floor(2^n/10), n>=5, is supported only when its top three
bits are zero. Write p=1/8 and m=n-3. After input reflection,
the inner gate is -g_m^+. Its mean is -mu with mu in [1/2,3/4].
Every inner coordinate with J>27/10 retains that bound after activation.
Every coordinate of the other type receives (7/8)Phi((2-d)^2,d^2)/d,
which is larger than the (3/4) correction above. Thus all inner
coordinates have B_i/d_i>27/10 at the outer root.

The top three coordinates are interchangeable activation controls.
Their influence is d=2a_n/2^n, and their ratio is psi(d). For every
n>=5, d lies in [3/16,1/5]. The saved 256-box rational logarithm
certificate proves, on that entire CLOSED interval,

    13/5 < psi(d) < 27/10.                          (7)

Therefore exactly the three top controls minimize. Since d->1/5,
continuity gives the exact limit

    psi(1/5)=(17 log2 17-56)/5.                     (8)

The slight rise then fall in the saved finite profiles is real; the
rounded 2.6974 plateau is not an exact equality at finite n.

### 5. Same limiting measure, different limiting minimum

Let m>=5, L=m^2, a_m=floor(2^m/10), n=m+L, and

    t_m=2^L a_m+1.

Compare f_m on m bits, extended by L dummies, with the new lex sign
on n bits. The new sign flips precisely one vertex, of probability
delta=2^(-n). Thus t_m/2^n=a_m/2^m+delta ->1/10.
Let s=v2(a_m). All L+s previously irrelevant terminal coordinates
become relevant and are interchangeable in the single-point terminal
core of size L+s. Its starting influence is d0'=2^(1-(L+s)), and its
starting nonconstant ratio is 2-2d0'. Above that core there are m-s
extensions of the form (4).

For d<=1/2 and |u|<=1, (1) gives

    Phi((u-c0)^2,d^2)/d <= d[4+2log2(1/d)].

Indeed (u-c0)^2+d^2<=17/4, whose log is <5/2, and c<3/2.
At h extensions the influence is d0'/2^h. Dropping the factor 1/2
in (4), summing sum 2^(-h)=2 and sum h2^(-h)=2, and bounding the
final constant-packet term by d0'[2(L+s)] yields

    2-2d0' <= B_i/d_i <= 2+d0'[6(L+s)+8].

Both right-hand error factors decrease as L+s increases. With
d0=2^(1-L), every newly relevant terminal coordinate therefore obeys

    2-2d0 <= B_i/d_i <= 2+d0(6L+8).                (9)

These bounds tend to 2. It remains to verify that old relevant
coordinates do not become cheaper.

For probability laws on a binary X and arbitrary finite Y, total
variation epsilon gives

    |H_p(X|Y)-H_q(X|Y)|<=h2(epsilon)+epsilon.         (10)

To prove it, write p=(1-epsilon)r+epsilon s and
q=(1-epsilon)r+epsilon t using the common part. Conditional entropy
of each mixture lies between its component average and that average
plus h2(epsilon). The two residual conditional entropies lie in [0,1].
This proves (10) without a dimension-dependent Y factor.

For a one-point sign edit of probability delta, Parseval and
Cauchy-Schwarz give TV(fhat^2,ghat^2)<=||fhat-ghat||_2=2sqrt(delta).
Each coordinate influence changes by at most 2delta by edge counting.
Every originally relevant coordinate has d_i>=2^(1-m), and by (7)
its old ratio exceeds 13/5. Write epsilon=2sqrt(delta), then use
h2(epsilon)<=epsilon log2(e/epsilon). Its new ratio is bounded below
by 13/5 minus an error at most

    2^((m-m^2)/2)[(m+m^2)/2+2]+3*2^(-m^2).        (11)

Here we replaced (26/5)delta by 6delta for the denominator change.
At m=5 expression (11) is <=17/1024+3/2^25<1/10; it decreases
thereafter (the polynomial factor grows by less than a factor two,
whereas its exponential factor shrinks by 2^(-m)). Thus every old
relevant coordinate has new ratio >5/2. The upper bound in (9) is
<5/2 for L>=25. Consequently the exact minimum is attained at the
L+s new terminal coordinates, has the closed sum (5)-(6), and tends
to 2. This is a counterexample to the unqualified limit statement,
not to FEI. Vanishing influence is precisely why dividing a continuity
bound by every coordinate's influence is invalid without a cutoff.

### 6. Biased raw recursive composition: exact coordinate profiles

Let z_j denote a child output sign, and let h=f_5 on five signs with
the bit convention from the target. It is antimonotone and its negative
event is

    (z_2=z_3=z_4=+1) AND (z_0=+1 OR z_1=+1).

For common child mean mu, write p+=(1+mu)/2 and p-=(1-mu)/2.
The next mean and the two derivative probabilities are

    mu_next=1-2 p+^3(1-p-^2),
    q_B=p+^3 p-, q_T=p+^2(1-p-^2).                 (12)

These are actual product probabilities, not biased Fourier influences
on fictitious physical bits. F_d remains unate in its physical leaves,
with orientation (-1)^d, and its singleton coefficient magnitude equals
its physical influence.

Here is the full unequal-mean composition formula. For target outer
block j, write h(g_0,...,g_4)=A(other blocks)+g_j B(other blocks).
For T subset the other four outer coordinates, expand about the mean:

    a_T=sum_{U superset T, j notin U} hhat(U) mu^(|U|-|T|),
    b_T=sum_{U superset T, j notin U} hhat(U+j) mu^(|U|-|T|),
    w_T=(1-mu^2)^|T|, q=sum_T w_T b_T^2.

With an inner coordinate influence d_i and singleton magnitude d_i,

    d_i'=q d_i,
    R_i'=q R_i+sum_{T nonempty} w_T
                    Phi((a_T+mu b_T)^2,b_T^2 d_i^2).           (13)

To prove it, a nonempty spectral remainder within target block j has
both paired coefficients equal to its inner coefficients times Bhat;
their contribution is q R_i. If that block's remainder is empty,
the adjacent pair is (Ahat+mu Bhat,Bhat*c_i). Specifying exactly which
other blocks have nonempty spectra leaves coefficients proportional
to the products of inner nonempty Fourier coefficients. Their squared
mass sums to w_T, proving homogeneity and (13). The T=empty pair is
exactly the new constant packet, removed in R_i'. This is a coherent
coefficient identity; it assumes no balanced child and no disjoint
support within a shared data block. All copies here are physically disjoint.

Equation (13), the mean recursion (12), and

    B_i'/d_i'=R_i'/d_i'+Phi(mu_next^2,(q d_i)^2)/(q d_i)

give closed finite recurrences for all leaf paths. There are 2^d
coordinate orbits at depth d, covering all 5^d leaves. The checker
calculates every orbit for d=1..4. Generic (13), before using |c_i|=d_i,
is independently checked by exact Walsh on all sixteen two-bit
Boolean inner functions, including biased and non-unate inputs;
direct biased-product moments reconstruct all a_T+mu b_T and b_T
at eight separate means, including the actual first three recursive means.

### 7. All-depth bound: raw recursion has a bounded good-coordinate path

Choose the leaf path T at depth one, then B at even ancestor depths,
T at odd ancestor depths. At depth one its ratio is <3. At depth two,
the exact outward certificate gives its full ratio <11/5. Its influence
is exactly

    d_2=(3/16)*(29/32)^3*(3/32)
       =219501/16777216 <1/64.                      (14)

We prove its later accumulated reserve remains bounded. At positive
mean write the minority probability s=(1-mu)/2; initially s=3/32.
The next negative-mean gate has positive probability

    u=1-(1-s)^3(1-s^2) <=3s+s^2 <(25/8)s <3/10.

The next positive-mean minority is

    s_next=u^4(2-u)<=2(25/8)^4 s^4<=s,

where 2(25/8)^4(3/32)^3<1 is an exact rational comparison. Thus this
alternating mean invariant holds at every finite depth. On the selected
path, q_B<=s<=3/32<1/8 and q_T<=2u^3<1/8, so influences shrink
by at least a factor eight at each subsequent level.

For any Boolean coordinate, d_i<=2 times the minority probability:
each pivotal edge in that direction uses one minority vertex. At a
positive mean, q_B=(1-s)^3 s>=(2/3)s>=d_i/3; at a negative mean,
q_T=u^3(2-u)>=u^3>=d_i^3/8. Thus in either state

    q>=d_i^3/8.                                   (15)

The sum of the first mass arguments in (13), including T=empty, is
E(A+mu B)^2=1-(1-mu^2)q by Booleanity of A+gB. The sum of second
arguments is q d_i^2, and their combined mass is <=1 since
d_i^2=c_i^2<=1-mu^2 by Parseval. Log-sum and (1) give a bound on
the normalized reserve increment:

    J_i'-J_i <=d_i log2(e/(q d_i^2))
             <=d_i[5+5log2(1/d_i)].                (16)

This is the line using actual Boolean normalization; arbitrary real
coefficient arrays would not supply the unit budget or variance bound.

At level h>=2, d_h<=(1/64)8^(-(h-2)). The right side of (16)
increases with d in this range. Summing the infinite geometric majorant
therefore bounds every later J by

    J_h < 11/5 + (5/64)sum_{r>=0}8^(-r)(7+3r)
        =11/5+65/98.

For h>=3, d_h<=1/512. The final constant packet has normalized cost
<=d_h[c+2log2(1/d_h)]<=5/128. Consequently

    B_i(F_h)/d_i(F_h) <11/5+65/98+5/128<3           (17)

for h>=3. The exact depth-one and depth-two certificates cover the
remaining cases. All finite means are strictly interior, so the selected
coordinate remains relevant. This proves E(F_h)<3 for every h, with
no finite-to-infinite extrapolation and no use of a balanced-child shortcut.

### 8. Honest boundary of the repair

The canonical lex limit and the raw composition route are now resolved
at their stated all-dimensional scopes. The weaker measure-only limit
is explicitly refuted by a second Boolean lex family. The first
unsupported line toward universal C018 is a coordinate-selection bound
for arbitrary Boolean residuals, or a witness-preserving admission
mechanism controlling every newly relevant low-influence coordinate.
Neither the raw family nor the threshold limit supplies that bound.
The root estimates do not establish restriction closure and cannot
discard any terminal entropy in a C019 application.

# A089 stage 3: sharp geometric signed-reserve repair

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All inputs below are independent uniform physical bits, all functions are Boolean signs, and all entropy is in bits. Write Phi(a,b)=(a+b)log2(a+b)-a log2(a)-b log2(b), with continuous zero conventions, and E(g)=min over relevant i of B_i(g)/Inf_i(g). The outer gate h is negative exactly on {0,1,2,4,8,16,32,6,17,40}; +1 is outer bit zero. F=h(g_1,...,g_6), with six disjoint identical copies of any nonconstant core g. There is no bound on the number of physical core inputs.

This repairs the specific failed admission step R_p(beta_i^2)>=0 for every core, and audits the dimension boundary of A072's finite repair. A sharp analytic criterion determines when every positive singleton size has nonnegative reserve. Its exact certificates prove that A072's selected orientation works for arbitrary-dimensional cores at every certified bias M/64 with minority M=4,...,32. The high-E hypothesis is unnecessary at those fixed biases. Neither a continuum policy nor unrestricted repeated composition follows.

## sources actually read, revisions and theorem locators

Read EXECUTION_PROTOCOL.json in full, revision 2026-10-11-execution-v2; BOARD.md supervisor snapshot 2026-10-13 23:59 UTC; B18 STREAM_NOTES.md v2 (unchanged 2026-10-11 23:34 UTC); own STATUS.md and LEARNED.md. B18's lex/raw-five-bit targets are completed in the prior run, so dispatch consumes the new A071/A072 signed-admission dependency rather than repeating them.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### Original failed statement and actual counterexample remain visible

False statement: for every actual core g and physical coordinate i, the recentered signed correction R_p(beta_i^2) is nonnegative; its sign-choice variant saying some common output sign always preserves E(g)>K is also false without a guard. The actual six-bit point defect has mean31/32, all nonconstant Fourier coefficients -1/32, and every influence1/32. Its E is greater than9/4. Both orientations under h have negative reserve and all 36 composed ratios less than9/4. The core and the matched outer automorphisms are transitive, so this covers every composed coordinate. out/FIXTURES.json independently encloses these inequalities. These are the A071 obstruction, independently reconstructed at that scope; no FEI counterexample is asserted.

### Exact signed split and physical coordinates

Put p=P(g=-1), m=1-2p, v=1-m^2. For any outer coordinate j, write h(y)=A(y_-j)+y_j D(y_-j). Expand A,D in the normalized product Fourier basis under independent output signs of mean m, obtaining coefficients a_T,d_T on subsets T of the five other blocks. Define U_T=(a_T+m d_T)^2, D_T=d_T^2, and q=sum_T D_T. The unfortunate reuse of the letter D in a section and its squared coefficients is resolved by the subscript. Physical pivotal enumeration gives q=2p(1-p)^3(2-p)>0. All six j have the same packet multiset by matched-pair permutations and pair swaps.

For a physical coordinate i of one selected core, let beta_i=ghat({i}), t=beta_i^2. Nonempty selected-core residual Fourier sets contribute q times their child Phi pairs, by homogeneity and Parseval on every other occupied block. For the empty selected-core residual set the two squared amplitudes are U_T and t D_T. Removing the inherited empty pair precisely once gives

    B_(j,i)(F)=q B_i(g)+R_p(t),
    R_p(t)=sum_T Phi(U_T,t D_T)-q Phi(m^2,t),
    Inf_(j,i)(F)=q Inf_i(g).

The influence identity follows independently from physical edges and the outer pivotal event. No unateness or singleton/influence equality is assumed. Irrelevant coordinates stay irrelevant; relevant ones stay relevant since q>0. Thus there is no lost new-coordinate sector in this disjoint substitution. The code independently reconstructs packets by uniform Walsh expansion and by direct signed conditional sections over32 outer assignments. It checks all six physical q's and sum U+vq=1 at each frozen bias.

### Sharp bias-only criterion

This scalar theorem applies to any nonnegative packet list with sum D=q>0, not just h. Terms with D=0 may be deleted. If m=0, R(t)>=0 directly. If m!=0 and any U=0 with D>0, define L=-infinity. Otherwise define

    L=sum_T D_T log2(U_T/D_T)-q log2(m^2),
    G=product_T (U_T/D_T)^(D_T/q).

Then R(t)>=0 for EVERY real t>0 if and only if L>=0. This is also the exact criterion on every interval0<t<=t_max with t_max>0.

Proof of sufficiency: for fixed t>0, z -> Phi(exp(z),t) is convex. Its second derivative is

    exp(z)/ln(2) * [ ln(1+t/exp(z))-t/(exp(z)+t) ] >=0,

using ln(1+x)>=x/(1+x). Homogeneity and weighted Jensen therefore give the quantitative lower bound

    R(t)>=q[Phi(G,t)-Phi(m^2,t)].

Phi(a,t) increases in a. L>=0 is exactly G>=m^2, proving sufficiency for every singleton size at once. For necessity, as t decreases to zero,

    Phi(a,t)/t = log2(a/t)+log2(e)+o(1)  (a>0).

Thus R(t)/t -> L whenever all active U are positive. A negative L yields negative reserve for all sufficiently small positive t. If some active U=0, their Phi terms are identically zero, while the subtracted packet still has its full q coefficient; R(t)/t tends to negative infinity. This proves necessity including coherent zero rows. When L=0, the Jensen bound still proves nonnegativity; equality for t>0 requires all active U/D equal m^2. No tolerance or float enters the criterion.

At a fixed dyadic bias p, arbitrarily small nonzero singleton sizes are realized by actual Boolean cores. On a sufficiently large n-bit cube, choose the number N of negative vertices to equal p2^n (even), put (N+2)/2 lex-first negatives in one coordinate half and (N-2)/2 in the other. Then |beta_i|=2^(2-n), tending to zero. Hence a negative criterion truly rules out all-dimensional coordinatewise reserve positivity at that bias, rather than merely ruling out an unrealizable scalar parameter. This observation makes no claim that these cores have high E.

### Exact arbitrary-dimension application at the certified biases

At p=M/64, M=1,...,63, the outward rational logarithm certificates prove L>0 exactly for

    M=4,...,15 and M=21,...,50.

M=32 is balanced and directly safe (it is included in the displayed range). All other unbalanced grid biases have L<0 or an active coherent zero row. Additional frozen biases1/128 and127/128 are unsafe. The full packet lists, exact L intervals, and any zero-row status are in out/BIAS_CERTIFICATES.json. These are classifications of the sharp scalar criterion, not sampled singleton sizes.

For any actual core of ANY dimension whose smaller output side has probability M/64, with4<=M<=32, first orient that smaller side to negative. Keep this orientation for M=4,...,15 or21,...,32; complement it for M=16,...,20. In the latter case the chosen count is64-M=44,...,48, in the certified safe range. This is a single sign for all physical coordinates. The criterion and the exact split imply

    B_(j,i)(F)/Inf_(j,i)(F) >= B_i(g)/Inf_i(g),
    E(F)>=E(g).

This extends A072's same-bias policy beyond six-bit cores and removes its E>=14/5 hypothesis on this bias grid. It does NOT extend to every core density with the same rounded count, every real p between grid points, or the bias produced at a subsequent composition depth. Nearby biases have continuity neighborhoods when all active U>0 and L>0, but no explicit continuum cover is certified here.

An explicit physical12-bit core with negative half-counts513 and511 has p=1/4, mean1/2, beta_0=-1/1024, and influence_0=1/1024. Its minority orientation has negative reserve; the complement at p=3/4 has positive reserve. This checks that the common orientation choice at M=16 is mathematically necessary for unconditional reserve positivity. No high-E claim is made for this core. Integer Walsh, Parseval and physical edges are checked directly on4096 entries.

### Terminal entropy retained and honest first unsupported line

Let pi be the normalized biased outer Fourier spectrum and rho(S)=ghat(S)^2/v on nonempty core sets. A physical Fourier set uniquely records its occupied-block pattern and its nonempty inner sets, so the law is hierarchical with disjoint support. Consequently

    H(F)=H_p(h)+6vq H(rho)=6q H(g)+C_p,
    C_p=H_p(h)-6q h2(v),
    I(F)=6q I(g).

The entire H(g) is retained. The proof does not pay C_p, choose a stopping policy, or reset the core reference. The first unsupported universal line is: at every dyadic bias arising from arbitrary-dimensional high-E cores, some common orientation has a nonnegative reserve for every relevant singleton, or any negative reserves can be paid from an exclusive signed terminal-inclusive influence reserve. The criterion at65 exact biases does not establish that line. Reapplying the fixed-grid policy to h(g^6) without checking its new bias is unsupported. Full FEI remains OPEN.

# A089 stage 4: one-use payment for the complete oriented composition ledger

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Reorient the output to its minority; this preserves its squared spectrum and influence. Write R(g)=H(g)/I(g) and Q for the total outer pivotal probability. Every level is finite-dimensional and Boolean.

The theorem proved here is, for EVERY finite depth d,

    -322/15 <= R(g_d)-R(g_0) <=1088/15<73.

The moderate prefix alone has signed correction in [-50/3,172/3], uniformly even when its duration grows without bound near the irrational bias fixed point. The rare tail has correction in [-24/5,76/5], improving A075's +16 bound. The exact starting entropy remains present. This is a composition ledger, not a physical restriction-tree payment or an absolute FEI bound.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### Original failed statement, counterexample and failed payment step

The false unguarded statement remains visible: every recentered reserve is nonnegative, or some common output sign always preserves E(g)>K. The actual six-bit point defect has E>9/4, while both common-sign36-bit compositions under h have every coordinate ratio<9/4. Our previous run's out/FIXTURES.json independently certifies it; that source is pinned again. The new A080 actual1296-bit circuit even has E>669/200 in the rare band and both next common signs fall below that threshold. Its circuit is source context, not independently re-reviewed here. These counterexamples do not refute FEI or A084's correctly restricted continuum theorem.

The payment gap being repaired is the unsupported step: finite termination of the moderate prefix supplies a dimension-independent bound by summing a fixed cost per level. Near the irrational fixed point the number of levels is not uniformly bounded. The replacement uses reciprocal influence, which decreases geometrically during the long segment, and charges each signed correction once. No uniform duration assertion is needed.

### Exact signed terminal identity

At an oriented input negative probability a, set m=1-2a, v=1-m^2 and

    q(a)=2a(1-a)^3(2-a), Q(a)=6q(a).

Let pi_T be the normalized biased Fourier masses of the actual gate, and let rho be the core's normalized nonempty Fourier law. A physical spectral set uniquely specifies its occupied blocks and the nonempty inner sets. Entropy chain rule and independent physical pivotal edges therefore give

    H(g')=Q(a)H(g)+C(a), I(g')=Q(a)I(g),
    C(a)=H_a(h)-Q(a)h2(v),
    R(g')-R(g)=C(a)/(Q(a)I(g)).                 (1)

Output complements affect none of these quantities. Every core's complete H(g), including its constant spectral mass, is retained. There is no discarded core remainder, hypothetical spectral law, or incoherent square of a signed sum. The code reconstructs biased outer masses from the actual integer Walsh table and verifies (1) against all14 nonconstant two-bit cores composed on12 physical bits.

### Scalar entropy envelopes

The outer spectral-set membership marginals are vq(a). Subadditivity and

    h2(vq)<=q h2(v)+v h2(q)

imply C(a)<=6v h2(q). The scalar inequality follows because the right side minus the left vanishes at q=0,1 and has second derivative -v(1-v)/[ln2(1-q)(1-vq)]<=0. Endpoint values follow by continuity. Also H_a(h)>=0 yields C(a)/Q(a)>=-h2(v)>=-1. Thus

    -1<=C/Q<=v h2(q)/q.                       (2)

We use h2(q)<=q log2(e/q). These are actual spectral entropy bounds, not an asserted entropy ratio bound for arbitrary cores.

For a minority probability p in [delta,1/2], delta=1/16, edge isoperimetry gives I(g)>=2p log2(1/p)>=1/2. The second inequality follows by concavity of2p log2(1/p) and its endpoint values1/2 and1. For completeness the first follows by entropy tensorization of1_A, A the minority set: Ent(1_A)=p log2(1/p), while averaged two-point fiber entropy is1/2 precisely on crossing fibers. Their sum is I(g)/2. Tensorization follows inductively from the entropy chain rule and log-sum convexity. Thus no FEI premise enters the influence normalization.

### Uniform payment for the long moderate segment

Put c=7/32. Ordinary moderate steps use a=p in [delta,c]. The pivotal polynomial has

    q'(p)=2(1-p)^2(2-10p+5p^2)>0

throughout this interval, since the last factor decreases there and equals53/1024 at c. Consequently

    Q(p)>=Q(delta)=313875/262144>rho=19/16.

Furthermore

    v/q=2/[(1-p)^2(2-p)]<=65536/35625<2.

Since h2(q)<=1, (2) gives -1<=C/Q<=2. Define the decreasing potential

    Delta_s=1/I(g_s)-1/I(g_(s+1))=(1-1/Q_s)/I(g_s)>0.

Because1-1/Q_s>=3/19, the exact increment (1) obeys

    -(19/3) Delta_s <= C_s/(Q_s I_s) <=(38/3) Delta_s.  (3)

Summing telescopes; it does not repeatedly spend the starting influence. Any consecutive ordinary segment, of any finite length, has total bill in

    [-(19/3)/I_start,(38/3)/I_start] subset [-38/3,76/3].

This also bounds every partial segment or a hypothetical infinite residence at the fixed point. Physical influence grows by at least rho each step. Finite termination alone was insufficient; reciprocal influence supplies the missing uniform payment.

### At most two complemented steps

Exact gate enumeration gives the minority updates

    tau(p)=12p^2-28p^3+27p^4-12p^5+2p^6 for p<=c,
    b(p)=p^4(3-2p^2) for p>c.

They are the actual minority branches: tau(c)<3/8 and b(1/2)=5/32<c. A possible INITIAL complemented step therefore enters the ordinary interval. After the ordinary segment either the core is rare, or its first crossing above c is at most tau(c)<3/8. One FINAL complemented step then has

    b(p)<=b(3/8)=7047/131072<delta.

Thus the entire moderate prefix has at most ONE ordinary segment and TWO complemented steps. Counting two long ordinary segments would unnecessarily weaken the payment.

At a complemented step a=1-p, c<p<=1/2. Its multiplier is

    Q(1-p)=12p^3(1-p^2)>=1003275/8388608>1/9.

The polynomial increases on this interval. Hence q>1/54 and, from (2),

    C/Q<=v log2(e/q)<=log2(e/q)<8,

using e<3 and54e<162<256. With I>=1/2, each complemented step has bill in[-2,16]. Combining with (3) proves the moderate prefix bound

    -50/3 <= total moderate correction <=172/3.       (4)

Every actual dyadic starting bias enters the rare band in finite time. On(0,c], tau is strictly convex, so tau(p)/p increases and has one positive fixed point p* between delta and c. The divided fixed-point polynomial is2p^5-12p^4+27p^3-28p^2+12p-1. The rational root theorem allows only +/-1,+/-1/2, all OUTSIDE that interval, so p* is irrational. Dyadic iterates cannot equal it. Below p* they eventually cross delta downward; above it they eventually cross c upward. This reproduces the termination argument, while (3) supplies the previously missing uniform price. In fact the ledger bounds hold for partial prefixes without using termination.

### Independent rare-tail payment, with a sharper upper constant

For0<p<=delta the minority policy uses a=p. The output minority tau(p) satisfies0<tau(p)<=12p^2<=3p/4: the positive event requires an unmatched pair among the12 unmatched input pairs. Also3p<=q(p)<=4p. Let the tail start at g_*, and set r_s=I(g_s)/p_s. Poincare I>=Var(g)=4p(1-p) gives r_0>=15/4, while (1) and tau<=12p^2 give

    r_(s+1)>=3r_s/2.                              (5)

Here is a direct proof of the improved upper envelope C(p)<342p^2. Let w=Var_p(h)=4tau(p)(1-tau(p))<=48p^2. There are at most63 nonempty outer spectral atoms, so

    H_p(h)<=h2(w)+w log2(63)
            <=48p^2 log2(63e/(48p^2)).

The last inequality uses h2(w)<=w log2(e/w) and monotonicity in w<=1. Meanwhile

    Qv=48p^2(1-p)^4(2-p)>=96p^2-432p^3,
    h2(v)>=v log2(1/v), log2(1/v)>=log2(1/(4p)).

Subtracting these positive lower bounds gives

    C(p)<=p^2[48 log2(63e/48)+192
                   +432p(log2(1/p)-2)]<342p^2.

Indeed63e/48<4, and p(log2(1/p)-2) is increasing up to delta and is at most1/8 there. Its derivative is log2(1/p)-2-log2(e)>0, since log2(e)<3/2. The elementary constants e<3 and ln2>2/3 follow from their positive series. This estimate uses no numerical bias sampling and no source theorem dependency.

For the lower envelope, in the biased expansion pi_T=v^|T| c_T^2, the raw coefficient c_T is the biased average of a mixed Boolean derivative. Such a derivative lies in[-1,1], so |c_T|<=1 and pi_T<=v^|T|. Therefore

    H_p(h)>=sum_T pi_T |T| log2(1/v)=Qv log2(1/v),
    C(p)>=-Q[-(1-v)log2(1-v)]>=-Qv log2(e).

This retains the distinction between the one-atom entropy -(1-v)log(1-v) and binary entropy h2(v); replacing h2(v) itself by v log2(e) would be false. Using v<=4p and log2(e)<3/2, (1) gives the signed tail envelopes

    -6/r_s <= C_s/(Q_s I_s) <=19/r_s.

Equations(5) and r_0>=15/4 sum a single geometric ledger:

    -24/5 <= total rare correction <=76/5.          (6)

The bound covers EVERY finite tail depth and counts each C_s exactly once. This independently proves and sharpens the entropy part of A075's tail bound; it does not re-review its erasure-loss constant.

### Complete result and honest universal boundary

Summing (4) and (6) gives, at every finite depth of the fixed policy,

    R(g_0)-322/15 <=R(g_d)<=R(g_0)+1088/15.

If the starting core is already rare, (6) alone applies. If the selected depth is inside the moderate prefix, (4) applies and implies the displayed wider bound. The exact bill is sum C_s/(Q_s I_s), and equivalently H(g_d)/(product Q_s)-H(g_0)=I(g_0) times that bill. Thus a single starting influence normalization suffices; no terminal entropy is erased. Every finite core is explicit by recursion, with no infinite Boolean function required.

The first unsupported universal line is an ABSOLUTE bound on the starting rare-core E or H/I, or a different operation whose surplus is paid from an independently certified terminal-core reserve. This fixed policy cannot turn a single finite seed into an unbounded H/I disproof: its entire orbit stays within the additive constants above. The theorem does not price arbitrary alternating uses of A072's one-flip escape, arbitrary shared-data insertions, or physical restriction trees. The warning about uncontrolled repeated excursions survives outside this named policy. Full FEI remains OPEN.
