# A089 stage 5: physical child-slack ownership and a retained-debit continuation

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

All functions are actual Boolean signs on finite uniform physical cubes; all entropies are in bits. S_K(f)=K I(f)-H(f) denotes a checked surplus, not a conjecturally available bank. Let h be negative on {0,1,2,4,8,16,32,6,17,40} and J(x)=-h(x xor1). Let g be the six-bit sign negative exactly on {0,1,2}. Form the actual36-bit function F=k(g^6), k=h or J, on disjoint copies. Query surviving inner coordinate i=0 or2 in block j=0 or1. These eight tags are alternative one-query prefixes, not eight debits simultaneously assigned to one owner.

Result: the four J alternatives have a certified K=4 child owner; the four h alternatives have one at K=9/2. After the other five core slacks pay each child's complete outer correction, the positive FULL query excess uses less than2/5 of the queried-child mean surplus. Its exact largest fraction among the eight alternatives is the J/block0/inner2 value, strictly between1/3 and2/5. A second physical query on that case's nonconstant core child preserves the old debit and pays old+new debit with less than3/5 of its grandchild surplus. The constant queried-core first child remains in the ledger with reach1/2 and selected-owner debit0; its full composed terminal retains all other cores and is nonconstant.

The generic owner rule below is proved for arbitrary independent cores and any fixed outer gate. Availability is an explicit hypothesis checked in these actual cases. No arbitrary-core surplus, universal FEI bound or full stopping-tree payment is asserted.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### Failed statement and counterexample kept visible

The tested A089 extension in FROZEN_SPEC.json was: ALL eight A072 cases can use a dedicated queried-child S4 owner, completely unused after the other five core slacks pay every child outer C. This is false. The first all-eight run failed at h/block0/inner0. More decisively its actual36-bit parent has

with exact enclosing endpoints in out/FAILED_OWNER.json and a display approximately-0.099323980511. ### Exact physical-query decomposition

For any outer Boolean gate k and independent cores g_l, let p_l=P(g_l=-1), v_l=4p_l(1-p_l), and q_l be the weighted outer pivotal probability. The unique occupied-block Fourier law gives

    H(F)=sum_l q_l H(g_l)+C(p),
    I(F)=sum_l q_l I(g_l),
    C(p)=H_p(k)-sum_l q_l h2(v_l).                    (1)

Query an actual uniform physical bit i in block j. Let superscripts b=0,1 denote its two children. Their true weights are1/2. Then q_j^b=q_j and average q_l^b=q_l for l!=j, because q_l is affine in p_j. Consequently

    Inf_(j,i)(F)=q_j Inf_i(g_j),
    Delta H(F)=q_j Delta H(g_j)+Delta C,
    Delta C=C(p)-average C(p^b).                     (2)

These identities retain every other core and both queried-core children, including constants. The mixed-bias coefficient computation uses raw multiaffine coefficients c_T=sum_(U superset T) khat(U) product_(l in U-T)(1-2p_l) and masses product_(l in T)v_l*c_T^2. It avoids divisions by v_l. At p_l=0 it still reconstructs the actual gate at all64 sign points by the full multiaffine Taylor identity; degenerate spectral masses vanish exactly. Direct weighted outer edge enumeration independently gives all six q_l. Actual inner Walsh transforms and edges establish the core/child data.

### Exact owner rule, including possible outer-cost deductions

For a proposed rate K, assume the relevant actual core surpluses are certified. In each child define

    A_b=q_j S_K(g_j^b),
    M_b=sum_(l!=j) q_l^b S_K(g_l)-C(p^b).

The COMPLETE child slack is A_b+M_b. Set U_b=A_b-max(0,-M_b); require U_b>=0. This pays any remaining outer cost from the selected child first, then identifies its unused part. The remaining other-owner amount is max(M_b,0), so child slack equals U_b+max(M_b,0) exactly. In our repaired cases M_b>0, hence U_b=A_b and the entire selected-child surplus is unused at admission.

Let e=Delta H(F)-K Inf_(j,i)(F)>0 and B=average U_b. A payment exclusively from this unused queried-child owner exists iff e<=B. This is the strongest exact rule for that prescribed owner: no allocation can average more than the available B. When B>0 and e<=B, choose theta=e/B and book D_b=theta U_b. Then average D_b=e, the remaining child owner is(1-theta)U_b, and all other-owner amounts remain untouched. The full parent slack is

    S_K(F)=average S_K(F^b)-e
           =average[(U_b-D_b)+max(M_b,0)]>=0.        (3)

Every complete terminal entropy is already present in these S_K values; none is removed to create the bank. The source identity (2) shows that e includes BOTH inherited core-query excess and Delta C. Paying only a positive curvature would leave a second debt unpriced.

### Eight exact feasible alternatives and their sharp fraction

Use K4 for J and K9/2 for h. For every one of the eight tagged queries, the independent certificates prove:

- every actual core and child has nonnegative S_K;
- M_0,M_1>0, so other cores pay each child outer correction before the selected owner is admitted;
- the FULL e is strictly positive;
- e<(2/5)B, hence at least3/5 of the selected mean owner remains;
- each full child and the full parent have nonnegative S_K.

The last parent inequality follows analytically from(3) and is also directly enclosed as an implementation check. The two positive Delta C cases are J/block0/inner0 and J/block0/inner2; the other six curvatures are negative. This agrees with A072 after independent reconstruction. All eight source full query decrements exceed4 times influence; increasing h's certified composition rate does not change that source assertion.

The exact minimum uniform fraction cap for these eight alternatives is lambda_*=e/B for J/block0/inner2, with e and B defined by(2)-(3), the explicit actual functions and rational biased spectra in out/CASES.json. Its outward interval is disjoint above all other fractions and proves1/3<lambda_*<2/5. Thus it is a defined exact entropy/logarithm ratio, not a rounded guessed constant; any smaller uniform fraction fails at this case. The sharpness is only for these rates, cases and prescribed unused owner. No optimal universal K or universal fraction is claimed.

### Carry the SAME booked debit through one more actual query

Select the root J/block0/inner2 alternative. Its bit0 child has the five-bit core g5 negative on{0,1,2}; its bit1 queried core is constant. The bit1 FULL composed function remains nonconstant and retains the other five core bills; CONTINUATION.json includes that unchanged full terminal explicitly. Let e1 be the root excess. Since only the nonconstant core child's selected slack is positive, the proportional allocation books D0=2e1 there and D1=0 on the constant-core branch. This is the actual reach-weighted debit: each root child has reach1/2.

Now query surviving inner coordinate0 in the nonconstant child, leaving the other five cores unchanged. Let U_parent=q_0 S4(g5), incoming remainder U_parent-D0, and U'_b=q_0 S4(g5^b), B'=average U'_b. Each new M'_b is again strictly positive, checked directly. Let e2 be this physical query's FULL rate4 excess. The certificate proves

    e2>0, D0+e2<(3/5)B'.                            (4)

Old debit is not reset: distribute it as D_old,b=D0 U'_b/B', and new debit as D_new,b=e2 U'_b/B'. Their conditional averages are EXACTLY D0 and e2. Each grandchild has unconditional reach1/4, while the unchanged root constant branch retains reach1/2 and debit0. At least2/5 of the grandchild selected owner survives both debits.

The no-renewal algebra is particularly explicit. Core slack transport gives

    B'-U_parent=q_0[Delta H(g5)-4 Inf_0(g5)],
    e2=q_0[Delta H(g5)-4 Inf_0(g5)]+Delta C2.

Subtracting, the actual remaining mean selected bank obeys

    B'-D0-e2=(U_parent-D0)-Delta C2.                (5)

The inherited core excess cancels precisely once; it is not booked again as a new outer allowance. The entire old D0 stays in the equation. Identity(5) is proved algebraically, and its independently calculated interval diagnostic encloses zero. The full child outer costs remain paid by the other owners throughout this prefix. out/CONTINUATION.json records every terminal, booked debit, reach and positive margin. This closes the supplied constant-child case through a second non-flat physical query, rather than merely postulating an owner at its first root.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### Failed statement and actual counterexample

For second-query bit 0, any third query at original coordinate 3, 4 or 5 gives the same numerical profile (all three are checked separately). Its zero child has

A constant queried core does not erase the other five cores or their entropy.

The related stronger statement “the genuinely unused queried-core owner alone still pays old plus new debit” also fails. Naively averaging raw U would leave a positive margin about 0.03183. That is misleading: U_0 must first pay -M_0. ### Definitions and exact surplus transport

For the fixed outer gate and independent cores, let q_l be its biased pivotal probabilities and C the complete composition entropy correction. The actual occupied-block spectrum gives

    H(F)=sum_l q_l H(g_l)+C,
    I(F)=sum_l q_l I(g_l).

For a query in block 0, q_0 is unchanged in both children and the remaining q_l average to their parent values. Define

    U_b=q_0 S(g_0^b),
    M_b=sum_(l!=0) q_l^b S(g_l)-C_b.

Consequently S(F^b)=U_b+M_b, with every full terminal included. If e=H(F)-average H(F^b)-4 Inf_i(F), then

    e=average S(F^b)-S(F).

The old debit D arrives at the current parent. The required outgoing mean booked debit is T=D+e, not e alone. These are exact identities; subtraction of interval enclosures is only a diagnostic check, not their proof.

### Strongest correct replacement for the prescribed two owners

Let w_b be fixed nonnegative child probabilities summing to one. Suppose the certified raw selected owner U_b>=0 and the signed remainder M_b give nonnegative corrected selected capacity

    V_b=U_b-max(0,-M_b)>=0,
    W_b=max(0,M_b).

Then V_b+W_b=U_b+M_b=S(F^b). Negative M has already been paid; W is the genuinely unused other owner. Put B=sum w_b V_b and W=sum w_b W_b. For any nonnegative required debit T, a payment from these two capacities exists if and only if

    T<=B+W.

Among such payments, the least possible mean draw on the other owner is exactly

    tau=max(0,T-B).

Necessity: selected draws total at most B, so other draws total at least T-B and are nonnegative. Sufficiency: spend s=min(T,B) from the selected owner and tau=T-s from the other owner. If B>0, book s V_b/B; when B=0 take all selected draws zero. If W>0, book tau W_b/W; when W=0 feasibility forces tau=0. Both coefficients lie in [0,1], so no child capacity is exceeded, and the conditional average booked debit is precisely T. This proof uses no independence or multiplication of conditional estimates. It is an exact deterministic allocation theorem. It also handles B=0, W=0, T=0 and equality T=B+W. If some V_b<0, this particular theorem is inapplicable; a negative owner cannot be silently clipped away.

### Carrying the actual old debit into the seven third-query alternatives

Let e1 be the root rate-4 excess. Its nonconstant queried-core child receives D0=2e1; the root's constant-core child, of reach 1/2, receives zero selected debit and is retained unchanged. Let e2 be the second-query full excess, let U^(2)_b be its two queried-core banks, and B2=(U^(2)_0+U^(2)_1)/2. Stage 5 establishes 0<D0+e2<(3/5)B2. The incoming debit at second child b is therefore

    D_b=(D0+e2) U^(2)_b/B2.

This is the SAME stage-5 allocation. It is computed anew from the exact source, not reconstructed from rounded saved decimals. For each active third query, calculate its full e3, then T_b=D_b+e3 and the corrected V,W above. In all seven cases D_b>0 and e3>0. To preserve the old and new labels separately, split each outgoing total debit d_c as (D_b/T_b)d_c and (e3/T_b)d_c. Their conditional means are exactly D_b and e3; no old label is erased when its owner changes.

For second bit 0, the active original coordinates are 3,4,5 (coordinate 1 is dummy). All three have tau>0 and

    1/4 < tau/W < 13/50.

The zero child has W_0=0 and V_0>0; the one child has V_1=0 and W_1>0. Spend V_0 in full in the zero child and book 2tau in the one child. The mean debit is B+tau=T. Thus the old debit crosses into the constant-core sibling's OTHER-core reserve; pretending it stayed solely in the selected core would be false. The zero child has zero remaining full surplus, the one child strictly positive remaining full surplus, and the mean remainder exceeds 0.1769. All full H and I remain present.

For second bit 1, all four original coordinates 1,3,4,5 are active. Both M children are positive, and 0<T<B. Book T V_b/B in each child; no W is spent. Both full remaining child banks are strictly positive.

Every new third-query terminal has unconditional reach 1/8. The root's unchanged branch keeps reach 1/2, and the untouched opposite second-query branch keeps reach 1/4 and its original debit. The seven alternatives are choices at their respective nodes; they are not seven simultaneous charges against one reserve.

Uniform selection and admissibility of those signed capacities is the unpaid universal line. Full FEI remains OPEN.

# A090 independent reconstruction

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Verdicts: HOLDS for A096-UNIFORM-SINGLETON-HALF-TAX v1, including its uniform endpoint coefficient, and HOLDS for A087-CONTROL-TREE-LAMBDA-KL-IDENTITY v1. Full FEI remains OPEN. These are the two targets explicitly assigned by B18 STREAM_NOTES v1 (2026-10-11 22:45 UTC). Neither is a near-complete universal FEI theorem. Entropy and KL below are in bits, Fourier coefficients normalized. Finite dimensions are arbitrary in the analytic proofs.

A096: genuine Boolean sign children on any finite residual cube with an interior product law; singleton selector X has independent bias p in (0,1). M=H(S_X|S_residual), J=I(X;T) in the physical-child Fourier experiment. With r=min(p,1-p), delta=1-sqrt(1-r), prove J-M<=h2(r)-h2(delta), uniformly in all residual parameters. The limit of the supremum normalized by p log2(1/p) is 1/2.

A087: any balanced Boolean core g, k>=1 fresh fair point controls, q=2^-k, F=1+A_k(g-1), and the physical control-first stopping tree. Prove the exact one-sided logarithmic-charge/magnitude-KL identity and sum <=4kq, preserving terminal entropy qH(g) and terminal influence qI(g).

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Independent two-dimensional proof of the singleton tax

Write a_T=hat G(T), b_T=hat H(T), with X=1 selecting G and X=0 selecting H. Define Q_T=p a_T^2+(1-p)b_T^2. Booleanity and Parseval ensure sum_T Q_T=1 and normalized physical child distributions. The two parent coefficients over T are

    c_T=p a_T+(1-p)b_T,
    d_T=sqrt(p(1-p))(a_T-b_T),
    c_T^2+d_T^2=Q_T.

For Q_T>0 the physical posterior X probability is z=p a_T^2/Q_T, and the spectral singleton probability is w=d_T^2/Q_T. Thus H(X|T)=sum Q_T h2(z) and M=sum Q_T h2(w). Zero packets disappear.

Let v=(sqrt(p)a_T,sqrt(1-p)b_T)/sqrt(Q_T), a real unit vector. The physical probabilities are the squared coordinates of v and the spectral probabilities those of Ov, where

    O=[[sqrt(p),sqrt(1-p)],[sqrt(1-p),-sqrt(p)]].

Set D=diag(1,-1), D'=O^t D O. The two coordinate imbalances are Z=v^t Dv=2z-1 and W=v^t D'v=1-2w. By direct multiplication, (D+D')^2=4p Id and (D-D')^2=4(1-p) Id. For any choices of signs, the operator norm of +/-D +/-D' is at most 2sqrt(1-r). Therefore

    |Z|+|W|<=2sqrt(1-r).

For t=min(z,1-z), s=min(w,1-w), this gives t+s=1-(|Z|+|W|)/2>=delta. Both t,s lie in [0,1/2]. If t+s<=1/2, binary entropy subadditivity h2(t)+h2(s)>=h2(t+s), and monotonicity up to 1/2 imply >=h2(delta). Subadditivity follows, for example, by putting two disjoint events of probabilities t,s on one probability space, applying entropy subadditivity to their indicators, and then taking their union. If t+s>=1/2, concavity gives h2(t)+h2(s)>=2(t+s)>=1>=h2(delta). Consequently every packet satisfies

    h2(z)+h2(w)>=h2(delta).

Average Q_T and use J=h2(p)-H(X|T) to obtain

    J-M<=h2(r)-h2(delta).

This is a universal analytic proof, without dimension limits or continuum sampling. The geometric step applies to every real pair of amplitudes, not specifically Boolean amplitudes. Booleanity is used to identify normalized probability experiments, and not to sharpen the packet inequality.

Since delta=r/2+r^2/8+O(r^3), direct entropy expansion gives

    h2(r)-h2(delta)
    =(r/2)log2(1/r)+[(log2(e)-1)/2]r+O(r^2 log(1/r)).

This uniform upper bound permits taking suprema before p->0. For the matching lower bound take H=1 and G=(-1)^(y1*y2) on two fair bits. Its four Fourier magnitudes are 1/2 and its mean is 1/2. The residual mixture has Q_empty=1-3p/4 and three other masses p/4. Hence J=H(Q)-2p=(3p/4)log2(1/p)+O(p). At the empty packet d^2=p(1-p)/4, so its contribution to M is (p/4)log2(1/p)+O(p). The three other packets have total mass 3p/4 and singleton probability 1-p, contributing (3p/4)h2(p)=O(p^2 log(1/p)). Therefore J-M=(p/2)log2(1/p)+O(p). This actual Boolean pair proves the matching supremum lower bound. The more general fixed-pair expansion in A096's other claim is not independently accepted by this report; it is unnecessary for this endpoint proof.

### 2. Independent control-tree proof

At an active node with s>=1 remaining controls put u=2^-s. Every control-indicator Fourier coefficient has magnitude u. Because E g=0, the Fourier coefficients with empty core are 1-u at the empty set and +/-u at nonempty control sets; those with core T!=empty are +/-u ghat(T). Pair coefficients without/with the next queried control. The empty packet has a=1-u,b=+/-u. Every other packet has a=+/-b. Parseval gives the queried influence sum b^2=u; the empty packet accounts for u^2. (Direct physical check: the other s-1 controls are active with probability 2u, and a balanced g disagrees with 1 with probability 1/2.) Thus

    lambda_s=sum b^2 log2((a^2+b^2)/b^2)
      =u-u^2+u^2 log2((1-2u+2u^2)/u^2).

For the actual children G=1+A_(s-1)(g-1) and H=1, the G empty coefficient is 1-2u>=0 and H empty coefficient is 1. Their magnitude mismatch is ((1-2u)-1)^2/4=u^2. For every other packet Hhat=0, and magnitude mismatch is Ghat^2/4=b^2. Therefore magnitude mismatch e_T equals b_T^2 in each packet, not merely in the total. The magnitude-mismatch residual

    u[log2(1/u)-D2(e/u || Q)]
      =sum e_T log2(Q_T/e_T)

is exactly lambda_s. Here Q_T=a_T^2+b_T^2 is a probability law and e/u is another probability law. Endpoints at s=1 satisfy the same calculation; zero packets are omitted.

The reach probability of this active node is 2^-(k-s)=q/u. Consequently

    sum w_s lambda_s=q[k+A_k],
    A_k=sum_(s=1..k)2^-s[2s-1+log2(1-2^(1-s)+2^(1-2s))].

For s>=1 the logarithm argument lies in [1/2,1]. Thus every bracket is nonnegative (the s=1 bracket is zero), and the bracket is <=2s-1. Since sum_(s>=1)2^-s(2s-1)=3, 0<=A_k<=3 and q(k+A_k)<=4kq. Consumed physical influence is exactly sum_s(q/u)u=kq. With probability q the terminal core is g and all other leaves are constant, so terminal entropy and influence are qH(g) and qI(g). No coordinate is reused on any physical path.

### 3. First unreconstructed universal implication

There is no unreconstructed line in either scoped target. The first missing line in a proposed universal conclusion is

    q H(g) <= C q I(g) uniformly over arbitrary balanced Boolean cores g.

After dividing by q>0 this is balanced FEI itself. It is not licensed by the proved control charges. Arbitrary f can be balanced as z*f on a fresh fair bit; its entropy is H(f), influence I(f)+1. Taking tensor powers before this balancing shows that a universal balanced bound implies H(f)<=C I(f) after division by tensor count and passage to that count's limit. Thus balanced FEI has full universal strength. The singleton tax supplies neither this terminal estimate nor a nonreusing influence payment for cumulative erasure. The authors' target statements explicitly preserve these limitations.

# A090 stage 2: independent low-influence reconstruction and the higher-level frontier

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

Verdict HOLDS for B20-ERASURE-LOW-INFLUENCE-COMPLETE v2 (A099). For any finite-valued s:N->[0,infinity) with s(N)=o(N), the supremum K_E(s) of aggregate spectral erasure B(F)/I(F) over all Boolean F satisfying 0<I(F)<=2^-s(N) equals the unrestricted supremum K_E, in the extended reals. No monotonicity of s, nonzero mean condition on the seed, finite universal constant, or exchange of an unproved remainder is assumed. The C018 corollary is C_FEI<=K_E(s)+2/ln2.

New A090 result, proved locally and pending independent review: for every fixed integer ell>=2, the same cutoff-class supremum of R_geell(F)/I(F) equals K_E, where R_geell is the sum of spectral edge entropy costs whose lower endpoint has cardinality >=ell. This retains the OPTIMAL aggregate erasure constant exactly, not just finiteness. All fresh parity coordinates of the hardening family have zero erasure and strictly positive influence, so the selected-coordinate minimum is exactly zero. This result concerns aggregate costs and does not transfer an existential good-coordinate witness.

Uniform cubes, actual Boolean signs, ordinary physical flip influence, entropy in bits, phi(x,y)=(x+y)h2(y/(x+y)), with continuous zero conventions. All dimensions in the proofs are arbitrary finite integers. Full FEI remains OPEN.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. Independent activation derivation and the checkpoint correction

Let h be any balanced Boolean core on d bits, k>=1, q=2^-k, A_k the all-zero control indicator, and F=1+A_k(h-1). Write core-frequency S and control-frequency U. The normalized Fourier coefficients are

    a_F(empty,empty)=1-q;
    a_F(empty,U)=-q for U nonempty;
    a_F(S,U)=q ahat(S) for S nonempty, all U.

Signs of control coefficients for another activated vertex do not change the squared spectrum. Edge entropy is homogeneous: phi(tx,ty)=t phi(x,y). At a core coordinate i, partition edges by residual core index T. For T nonempty, all 2^k control slices reproduce q^2 times the core cost. At T empty, the core cost was phi(0,ahat(i)^2)=0, but the nonempty control slices have masses q^2 and q^2 ahat(i)^2, and the empty slice has masses (1-q)^2 and q^2 ahat(i)^2. Thus exactly

    B_data(F)=qB(h)
       +q(1-q)sum_i phi(1,ahat(i)^2)
       +sum_i phi((1-q)^2,q^2 ahat(i)^2) >=qB(h).       (1)

A090's provisional checkpoint equality omitted the first correction sum. Comparison with A099 exposed the omitted pure-control slices, and CHECKPOINT_CORRECTION.md immediately saved the correction. A099's displayed formula includes both sums and needs no repair. The statement-only LOWER BOUND, and therefore the independently reconstructed theorem, survive. This report uses the corrected exact identity throughout; no claim of complete correct checkpoint algebra is made.

For each control coordinate j, the exceptional pair is ((1-q)^2,q^2), and every other occupied pair has equal squared masses. Queried influence is q, with q^2 in the exceptional pair. Therefore

    B_j(F)=2q(1-q)+phi((1-q)^2,q^2),  Inf_j(F)=q.    (2)

Each core influence is q times its original value. Direct pivotal counting gives the same formula, since the other k-1 controls are active with probability 2q and balanced h is negative with probability 1/2. Consequently

    I(F)=q[I(h)+k].                                  (3)

### 2. A099 v2 independently reconstructed, including oscillatory cutoffs

For nonconstant f on n>=1 bits, take m disjoint copies and h=z product_b f(x^(b)) with one fresh parity bit. The squared Fourier law is a product, so B(h)=mB(f), I(h)=mI(f)+1 and E h=0. Equations (1)-(3) imply

    B(F)/I(F)>= mB(f)/(mI(f)+1+k).                    (4)

Here is an independent choice of k that handles arbitrary oscillations in s without the author's initial-segment maximum. Let

    eta_m=sup_{N>=mn} s(N)/N.

Because s(N)/N->0 and every value is finite, eta_m is finite and tends to zero. For any fixed parity count ell>=1 choose

    k_m=ceil[4(n+1)m sqrt(eta_m)+2sqrt(m)],
    N_m=mn+ell+k_m.                                  (5)

Then k_m=o(m), k_m<=m eventually, and ell<=m eventually, so N_m<=(n+2)m. Since N_m>=mn and eta_m<=1 eventually,

    s(N_m)<=eta_m(n+2)m
           <=4(n+1)m sqrt(eta_m),
    k_m-s(N_m)>=2sqrt(m).                            (6)

Also I(f)<=n and mI(f)+ell+k_m<=(n+2)m eventually. Since log2((n+2)m)=o(sqrt(m)), (6) gives

    0<I(F_m)=2^-k_m[mI(f)+ell+k_m]<=2^-s(N_m).        (7)

Taking ell=1 in (4)-(7), the lower ratio tends to B(f)/I(f). Therefore K_E(s)>=B(f)/I(f) for every nonconstant seed. Supremizing gives K_E(s)>=K_E; the opposite inequality holds by class inclusion. This argument also handles infinite K_E: for every arbitrarily large finite seed ratio, an admissible sequence approaches it. No finiteness assumption or unproved exchange of limit and supremum is used. This establishes A099's exact v2 statement and, by the already reconstructed C018 comparison, its C_FEI corollary. Constants have B=I=0 and need no ratio.

### 3. New exact tail transport, all fixed ell>=2

Replace the one balancing bit by a fresh parity of ell>=2 bits:

    h=chi_Z product_b f(x^(b)),  |Z|=ell.

Every occupied Fourier set of h contains all of Z. Thus h is balanced and all its singleton Fourier coefficients vanish. Its aggregate erasure is still mB(f), its influence is mI(f)+ell, and its fresh parity coordinates have zero erasure. In (1) both correction sums now vanish exactly:

    B_data(F)=q mB(f).                               (8)

For an original seed coordinate, every nonzero-cost data edge has both endpoints containing Z. Its lower endpoint therefore has size at least ell, in every control slice. Each fresh parity coordinate has no adjacent occupied Fourier sets, including the exceptional empty-frequency row, because ahat(i)=0. Hence

    R_geell,data(F)=q mB(f),
    B_z(F)=0, Inf_z(F)=q>0 for every z in Z.          (9)

No parity frequency is treated as a change of physical basis: these are actual fresh coordinates and their influence overhead is explicitly retained.

The control part has a particularly simple exact tail count. Pairs with nonempty core S contribute kq in total and all have lower endpoint size >=ell. Pairs with empty core and a lower pure-control subset of size r>=ell have equal masses q^2. Their number is k*binom(k-1,r). Therefore

    R_geell(F)=q mB(f)+kq
      +2kq^2 sum_(r=ell..k-1) binom(k-1,r),           (10)

where the sum is zero for ell>k-1. In particular

    B0(F)=k phi((1-q)^2,q^2),
    T12(F)=2k(k-1)q^2,
    R_ge2(F)=q mB(f)+2kq(1-kq).                      (11)

Formula (11) also follows by subtracting the exact first two layers from (2),(8). It applies to every ell>=2, every actual seed, all m,k>=1. The extra cost is nonnegative because k2^-k<=1/2.

For nonconstant f, taking k_m from (5) yields admissible cutoff functions, k_m/m->0 and

    R_ge2(F_m)/I(F_m)
       =[mB(f)+2k_m(1-k_m2^-k_m)]
          /[mI(f)+ell+k_m] -> B(f)/I(f).             (12)

For general fixed ell, (9) gives the same lower limiting ratio and R_geell<=B supplies the upper supremum comparison. Thus, defining K_geell(s) over the cutoff class,

    K_geell(s)=K_E(s)=K_E (extended reals).           (13)

The fresh parity bits prove at the same time

    min_(i:Inf_i(F)>0) B_i(F)/Inf_i(F)=0,             (14)

exactly, independent of the seed aggregate ratio. This prevents falsely identifying (13) with a selectable-coordinate-hardness assertion. Formula (13) sharpens the assembly's additive layer comparison: deleting any fixed number of low Fourier layers and imposing arbitrary subexponentially small influence preserves the OPTIMAL aggregate-erasure supremum without additive loss. This is an extraction of full-FEI-strength hardness, not a payment theorem.

### 4. First unreconstructed universal implication

The source v2 theorem is reconstructed completely. The assembly's first unsupported universal payment is its explicit OPEN-C018-HIGHER conjecture:

    exists one finite K, for every actual Boolean f:
        R_ge2(f)<=K I(f).                            (OPEN)

No argument in the assembled manuscript closes it. The new (13) shows even its restriction to the low-influence cutoff class has optimal constant K_E. A097's reference identities, paid B0/T12 layers, and existing bounded-ratio B05 example do not constitute this missing all-dimensional payment. No full-chain acceptance of A099's separate B05 certificate application, A097 reference application, or history-specific C024 is issued here. The review accepts exactly the low-influence fragment and honestly isolates the remaining universal line.

SOURCE agents\A090\runs\20261014T010003Z-fleet05-A090-independent-reconstruction\REPORT.md
# A090 stage 3: the terminal refund cannot be compressed to degree

## exact target, quantifiers, hypotheses and units

The live A099/A096 frontier is a signed-Boolean terminal-core KL lower bound paying the same complete conditional-edge bill with one physical influence reserve. Its normalized refund identity is already independently reconstructed by A073 and A099, so this stage does not repeat that baseline or the retired five-bit census/half-tax audit. It audits a new downstream compression mechanism: replacing each full derivative/reference row divergence by its divergence after retaining only the residual Fourier degree.

New result A090-DEGREE-COARSENED-REFUND-OBSTRUCTION v1, author-proved and pending independent review: this compression cannot yield a dimension-independent retained-price bound, even for actual Boolean tribes whose full retained price is at most six times influence.

For every integer w>=4 let m=2^w, p=1/m, beta=1-p, n=mw, and take

    f_w=1-2 product_(b=1..m)(1-A_w(x_b)),

where all width-w blocks are disjoint and A_w is the all-zero point conjunction. The sign is +1 on the OR of the conjunctions. For a relevant coordinate i set

    epsilon_i=Inf_i(f), mu_i(T)=p_(T+i)/epsilon_i,
    r_i(T)=p_T+p_(T+i),
    bar_mu_i(t)=sum_(|T|=t)mu_i(T),
    bar_r_i(t)=sum_(|T|=t)r_i(T).

Both mu_i and r_i are normalized row probability laws. Every entropy and KL below is in bits; p_S=fhat(S)^2, ordinary physical influence is I=sum epsilon_i. Define

    M=sum_i epsilon_i[log2(1/epsilon_i)-D2(mu_i||r_i)],
    M_degree=sum_i epsilon_i[log2(1/epsilon_i)-D2(bar_mu_i||bar_r_i)].

For the actual family, every epsilon_i=epsilon=2p beta^(m-1), I=2w beta^(m-1), and

    0<=D_degree<=3+log2(w+1/2),
    M_degree/I>=w-4-log2(w+1/2).

In particular M_degree/I>=w/2 for all w>=32, while 0<=M<=B<=H<=6I for all w>=4. The discarded within-degree KL refund per influence is at least w/2-6 for w>=32 and diverges. This is a mechanism obstruction, not a disproof of FEI and not a no-go for full function-specific conditional-edge references.

## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

### 1. The normalized identity and exactly what coarsening loses

For any actual Boolean f and relevant i, sum_T r_i(T)=1 and sum_T mu_i(T)=1. Direct expansion of KL gives

    epsilon_i[log2(1/epsilon_i)-D2(mu_i||r_i)]
      =sum_T p_(T+i) log2((p_T+p_(T+i))/p_(T+i)).     (1)

Thus M is the one-sided downward edge charge, not a new unpriced information term. Each summand is nonnegative. Its complementary upward term gives the binary edge entropy, so M<=B. Conditioning reduces entropy and the entropy chain rule give B<=H. No independence of spectral bits is assumed.

Applying the KL chain rule to the deterministic map T->|T| gives

    D2(mu_i||r_i)=D2(bar_mu_i||bar_r_i)
      +sum_t bar_mu_i(t)
          D2(mu_i(.|t)||r_i(.|t)).                  (2)

Consequently

    M_degree-M=sum_i epsilon_i sum_t bar_mu_i(t)
                              D2(mu_i(.|t)||r_i(.|t))>=0.  (3)

This is also an honest complete normalized row reference: on positive bar_mu degrees define tilde_r_i(T)=bar_r_i(t)mu_i(T)/bar_mu_i(t); on zero bar_mu degrees choose any normalized conditional law. Its degree marginal is bar_r_i, total mass is one, it is positive wherever mu_i is positive, and D2(mu_i||tilde_r_i)=D2(bar_mu_i||bar_r_i). It is function-dependent, so the existing influence-only reference obstruction does not by itself settle this case. Equation (3) identifies its actual lost conditional refund.

### 2. Actual Boolean spectra and exact degree laws

Each factor b=1-A_w has Fourier coefficient beta at the empty set and -p at every nonempty set, with squared norm beta. Its normalized squared spectrum has probability beta at the empty set and p/(m-1) at each nonempty set. Equivalently a block is active with probability p; conditional on activity its subset is uniform nonempty. Write xi_w for that active block's degree distribution:

    xi_w(t)=binom(w,t)/(2^w-1), 1<=t<=w.

Let nu be the degree law of the product of m normalized factor spectra. Its PGF is

    [beta+p*((1+z)^w-1)/(2^w-1)]^m.                 (4)

The number L of active blocks is Bin(m,p), with E L=mp=1, and given L its degree is the sum of L independent xi_w variables. For the Boolean OR sign, its squared degree masses W_t are

    W_0=(1-2beta^m)^2,
    W_t=4beta^m nu_t for t>=1.                       (5)

This Fourier formula is an exact product expansion of an actual sign-valued function, not a relaxed spectral vector. Each coordinate's pivotal event requires the other w-1 bits in its block to activate and all other blocks to be inactive, so

    epsilon=2p beta^(m-1), I=n epsilon=2w beta^(m-1). (6)

The group of within-block and block permutations is transitive on coordinates and preserves degree. It does not act transitively on every fixed-degree subset. Coordinate transitivity alone yields

    bar_mu(t)=(t+1)W_(t+1)/I,
    bar_r(t)=(1-t/n)W_t+((t+1)/n)W_(t+1),
       0<=t<=n-1.                                  (7)

In particular, for t>=1,

    bar_r(t)>=4beta^m(1-t/n)nu_t.                    (8)

The derivative degree law has another independent representation. Conditioning a selected coordinate to be in the squared spectrum forces its block and makes the other w-1 coordinates of that block uniform independent Fourier bits. Other blocks retain their normalized b spectra. Thus

    T_mu=U+sum_(b=1..m-1) V_b,
    U~Bin(w-1,1/2),
    P(V_b=0)=beta;  P(V_b=t)=p xi_w(t), t>=1.

All variables in this representation are independent. It gives exactly

    bar_mu(0)=2p beta^(m-1)=epsilon,
    E T_mu=(w-1)/2+(m-1)p*w/(2beta)=w-1/2.           (9)

### 3. A pointwise domination that retains the true degree marginals

For t>=1, the nonzero U masses obey

    P(U=t)/xi_w(t)=2beta*(w-t)/w<=2beta.

The zero U mass is 2p. If U=0, the active-block count from the other m-1 blocks is Bin(m-1,p). Its likelihood ratio against Bin(m,p) at count j is (m-j)/(m beta)<=1/beta. This component of bar_mu is bounded pointwise by (2p/beta)nu.

For the U>0 component, replace U's subprobability by 2beta times xi_w and force one active block. The resulting total count L'=1+Bin(m-1,p) has likelihood ratio j/(mp)=j against Bin(m,p). Every active block has degree at least one; on total degree t its count j is at most t. Therefore

    bar_mu(t)<=(2p/beta+2beta t)nu_t, t>=1.           (10)

This inequality does not assert uniformity of spectra within degree. It arises from the exact conditional block-count distributions, and will be used only for the coarsened row.

### 4. Uniform analytic KL bound, without numerical entropy estimates

For w>=4, elementary logarithm inequalities give

    1/4<=beta^m<=e^-1<3/8,
    e^-1<=beta^(m-1)<=1/2.                           (11)

For the lower bound use ln(1-p)>=-p/(1-p), whence beta^m>=exp(-m/(m-1))>=exp(-4/3)>1/4. The last comparison follows from ln2>2/3. The upper bound uses ln(1-p)<=-p and e>8/3. For beta^(m-1)<=1/2, expand (1+1/(m-1))^(m-1)>=2; the lower bound follows from the same logarithm inequality. Both ln2>2/3 and e>8/3 follow from positive elementary series, not numerical approximations.

By (5), bar_r(0)>=W_0>=1/16. Equations (9),(11) give bar_mu(0)=epsilon<=p<=1/16. Therefore the zero-degree contribution bar_mu(0)log2(bar_mu(0)/bar_r(0)) is nonpositive.

On the head 1<=t<=n/2, combine (8),(10),(11). Since beta>=3/4 and p<=1/4,

    bar_mu(t)/bar_r(t)
      <=(2p/beta+2beta t)/(2beta^m)
      <=4/3+4t<=4(t+1).                             (12)

Thus the entire head contribution is at most

    2+E log2(T_mu+1)<=2+log2(w+1/2),                 (13)

by (9) and Jensen. Dropping negative terms was only done using valid upper bounds; no positive-only truncation is substituted for KL.

For the tail, (9) and Markov give P(T_mu>n/2)<=2/m. The actual row law always obeys r_i(T)>=epsilon mu_i(T), and therefore also bar_r(t)>=epsilon bar_mu(t). Hence its log ratio is bounded above by log2(1/epsilon). From (6),(11),

    w-1<=log2(1/epsilon)<=w-1+log2 e<w+1/2.

Here log2 e<3/2 follows from ln2>2/3. The tail contribution is at most

    2(w+1/2)/2^w<=9/16<1, w>=4,                    (14)

as (w+1/2)/2^w decreases for integer w>=1. Adding (13),(14) and the nonpositive zero term proves

    D_degree<=3+log2(w+1/2).                         (15)

Normalization proves D_degree>=0. Since every coordinate has the same row and influence,

    M_degree/I=log2(1/epsilon)-D_degree
                >=w-4-log2(w+1/2).                 (16)

For all real w>=32, log2(2w)<=w/4: it holds at32, and its right-minus-left derivative is 1/4-1/(w ln2)>0 thereafter. As w+1/2<=2w and 4<=w/4, (16) is at least w/2 for every integer w>=32. This is an all-w proof of divergence on actual Boolean functions, not extrapolation from the finite certificates.

### 5. The complete retained cost stays bounded

For a nonempty Fourier set hitting j blocks, its squared mass is 4p^(2j)beta^(2(m-j)). Under the normalized product law in (4), E j=mp=1. Expanding the atom entropies gives the exact formula

    H=h(W_0)+8w beta^m
      -8beta^m log2(beta)[m(1-beta^m)-1]
      -8beta^m(1-beta^m),                            (17)

where h(x)=-x log2 x. Since h(W_0)<=1, m(-log2 beta)<=[m/(m-1)]log2 e<8/5 for m>=16, and the last term is nonpositive,

    H<=8w beta^m+13beta^m+1.

Divide by I=2w beta^(m-1), use beta<=1 and beta^(m-1)>=e^-1>1/3, and obtain

    H/I<=4+8/w<=6, w>=4.                            (18)

Together with (1), M<=B<=H proves 0<=M<=6I. By (3),(16), for w>=32 the normalized discarded conditional refund satisfies

    (M_degree-M)/I>=w/2-6 ->infinity.                (19)

Thus the exact full conditional-edge KL contains a growing within-degree refund. The entire row degree distributions are function-specific and exact, yet retaining them alone still loses the payment. No influence-only or arbitrary-real-vector premise appears.

### 6. The first unsupported implication in the live universal bridge

The first universal line remains

    D2(P_joint||Q_joint)>=Gamma(f)-K,
    Gamma(f)=I(f)^-1 sum_i epsilon_i log2(1/epsilon_i),

with the actual normalized derivative/reference joint laws and an absolute K. It is equivalent to a universal M<=KI bound, hence FEI-strength through C018. The tempting replacement of the full divergence by its degree marginal is FALSE: (16) defeats every finite K.

Neither A096 nor A099 asserts this false replacement. Their existing identities and scoped acceptances remain valid, and the actual full divergence is not refuted by this family. The required contribution is a single owned bound retaining the conditional within-degree term in (2) together with terminal entropy. Paying each degree independently or keeping only its scalar mass is not licensed by data processing.

All entropies, cross-entropies and divergences are in bits. Functions are actual Boolean signs on finite uniform physical cubes. Influence means ordinary flip influence. A reference Q is normalized and positive wherever the actual squared Fourier law P is positive; reference-only mass and the empty Fourier set are retained. Only fresh physical coordinates are queried, with fair outcome probabilities, on a finite possibly adaptive tree. Children inherit the same reference by marginalization unless an explicit reset cost is charged.

The finite fixture is the nine-bit sign F(y,x)=b(y)+1[y=0](Maj3(x)-1), with six data bits, three fresh majority bits, and b positive exactly when its first three data bits are zero. Its original core influence quota is 4 I0=3, with I0=(I(b)+I(b-2*1_origin))/2=3/4. The incoming reference for the stronger conclusion below is Q=P_F, accompanied by the actual Fourier phases. ## complete derivation or exact first unsupported line

Let a_T and a_(T+j) be actual signed parent amplitudes and put p_0=a_T^2, p_1=a_(T+j)^2, m=p_0+p_1. The child probabilities at T are (a_T+a_(T+j))^2 and (a_T-a_(T+j))^2. Their average is m. Booleanity ensures each child law is normalized. This is a physical restriction statement, not spectral conditioning.

Put q_0=Q(T), q_1=Q(T+j), q=q_0+q_1. The full conditional occupancy divergence is

R_j = sum_T sum_(e=0,1) p_e log2(p_e q/(m q_e)),

with zero-p terms omitted. For m>0, q>0 by finite CE. On each such fiber the two normalized conditional distributions are (p_e/m) and (q_e/q), so R_j is a sum of m-weighted KL divergences and is nonnegative. All Q-only mass remains in q. Gibbs' inequality follows from ln t<=t-1 applied on the positive-p support, including the possible missing reference mass. No independence across fibers or coordinates is assumed.

Direct expansion, fiber by fiber, gives

CE(P,Q) - CE(P_minus_j,Q_minus_j) = B_j(P)+R_j.

Cross-entropy is linear in its first argument. Since the average actual child law is P_minus_j, this is exactly

CE(P,Q) = B_j + (CE(P_plus,Q_minus_j)+CE(P_minus,Q_minus_j))/2 + R_j.   (1)

Multiplying (1) by each node's actual reach probability and summing over a finite adaptive tree cancels every internal CE term once. Therefore

sum_v w_v B_v + sum_leaf w_leaf CE(P_leaf,Q_leaf) + sum_v w_v R_v = CE(P_root,Q_root).   (2)

This proves the abstract A096 continuation theorem, including unequal stopping depths, constants, spectral zeros and retained full terminals. Its priced application follows by substituting the supplied A097 root inequality CE_root<=(2C0+10) I(F); no new estimate of that floor is needed or inferred. A reference chosen as the true child law has zero KL, but using that choice does not establish a cheap universal root bound.

For the A001 fixture, the complete root spectrum has one atom 2401/4096, seven atoms 225/4096, 56 atoms 1/4096, 256 atoms 1/16384, and 192 zeros. These counts sum to 512 entries and the probabilities sum to one. Consequently

H(F)=385/32-(2401/1024)log2(7)-(1575/2048)(log2(3)+log2(5)) < 489/200.

The strict inequality has a rational log-interval certificate in code/check.py. In particular 3-H(F)>111/200. Substituting Q_root=P_F into (2) proves, for **every** finite adaptive stopping tree on this particular fixture,

sum_v w_v B_v + sum_leaf w_leaf [H(P_leaf)+D(P_leaf||Q_leaf)] < 489/200 < 3.   (3)

Thus the same original quota pays all already executed prefix erasures, every continuation erasure, and both full terminal reference debts. The original unused margin remains greater than 111/200; each accumulated R only increases the margin. No fresh bank is created at a child. The universal quantifier over trees in (3) follows from the analytic identity, not an enumeration of all tree policies. A separately pre-booked charge d can be included only if its own remaining-budget condition d+H(F)<=3 is verified.

The six A001 data-first, majority-next policies reproduce its stated narrow bounds. For active data queries, the actual entropy-terminal margin exceeds 1.112678 and the frozen-escrow margin lies between -0.028552 and -0.028551. For dummy data queries, the corresponding margins exceed 0.634309 and lie between 0.546819 and 0.546820. Keeping the inherited terminal CE instead of terminal entropy still leaves margins greater than 0.568250 (active) and 0.571248 (dummy). These are paid from one original bank, with the actual encoder interaction already present in the actual restriction spectra.

The constant-child obstruction in A096 is also reconstructed exactly. The actual three-bit table [1,1,1,1,-1,1,1,1] with matching initial Q has, after querying bit0 and taking outcome1, a constant +1 child. Its inherited Q is (5/8,1/8,1/8,1/8), so its influence is zero but its terminal CE is log2(8/5)>0. This rejects a bound on every inherited leaf CE by that leaf's own influence. It does not reject the ancestor budget (2) or all possible paid resets.

The first unreconstructed source clause in A001 is: "The actual Maj3 encoder erasure differs positively from the pending shadow-plus-inherited reservation." The statement does not define that reservation as a formula in the restricted amplitudes, so the signed difference is not a uniquely specified quantity to check. The actual encoder erasures are included in W throughout our reconstruction, and the stated payment margins require no such comparison. This is a precise limit of this statement-only review, not a refutation of the subsidiary clause or an invitation to treat it as proved.

The first unpaid universal line is the replacement of a paid, inherited reference by a fresh cheap reference on an arbitrary non-flat child while preserving the one unused ancestor account. For prescribed fresh child references Q'_s define Delta_s=CE(P_s,Q'_s)-CE(P_s,Q_minus_j). Then the exact reset ledger is

B_j + avg_s CE(P_s,Q'_s) = CE(P,Q)-R_j+avg_s Delta_s.   (4)

A local transfer therefore needs avg Delta_s<=R_j plus a specifically identified unused reserve. Neither (1), the scoped A097 floor, nor the finite fixture certifies that inequality for every arbitrary Boolean child. More generally the existence for every f of a normalized Q with CE(P_f,Q)<=K I(f) is equivalent to FEI itself: H(P)<=CE(P,Q), and conversely Q=P gives equality. Treating that root premise as already paid would be circular. No unsupported implication is found within A001's explicitly quantified payment conclusions or A096's abstract no-reset identity; the universal fresh-floor admission remains unsupported, separately from A001's undefined subsidiary comparison.
